Doc-comment updates: `RenderEngineFlags.h:17-44` (false = workers park, not "serial loop"); `PluginProcessor.cpp:1918-1930` (no more branch); `StandaloneEditor.cpp:5152-5210` (toggle now parks workers). Menu items 202 (toggle) + 203 (Run MT Diagnostic) + the `<MultiCoreRendering>` `settings.xml` persistence kept intact.
  - **2d — VibeGraph half (the cleanup the struck QA-Ea Part B Task 2 left behind):** verifying the PluginProcessor half surfaced the deeper serial-dead chain — the legacy monolithic `VibeGraph::processBlock` (:1502-1586) had ZERO call sites (only comments); its caller was the deleted serial tail; MT runs the task graph. Dead with it: `mChannelAccum` + `getChannelAccumulator` + `clearChannelAccumulators` (MT uses arena/pull, not the accumulators); `processAuxInserts` (zero callers; MT does aux via `PassiveStripTask`); scratch `mLayersBuf`/`mBassBuf`/`mDrumsBuf`/`mSumBuf` + the prepare-time sizing; the `clearChannelAccumulators()` call at `PluginProcessor.cpp:1700`. `processBus`/`processMasterBus` SURVIVE (MT tasks call them); SC-recv buffers SURVIVE (`pullSidechainPredecessorsToGraph` populates them under MT).

- **Task 3 — folded Both-cleanups on the surviving MT path** (the SC-cleanup-scope items): removed dead `BlockContext.busAnySolo` field + the dispatch-body `soloOfBus`/`busAnySolo` compute + `PassiveStripTask`'s solo-flag switch + the now-redundant `processBus(... anySolo ...)` parameter — verified safe because `processBus` self-computes `anyBus = anyBusSoloed()` and `ignoreUnused`'d the caller arg (QA-Ea Part A), passing `anyBus` to every bus case including FX (`processEffectsBus(buf, bpm, anyBus, panLaw)`). The FX-bus solo path (`EffectsBusNode` `busAnySolo` param) untouched — it receives the self-computed `anyBus`. GUARDRAIL (§9 nineteenth Forks): no strip-level solo re-introduced into bus gating. Single `processBus` caller (`PassiveStripTask`) updated; signature now `(busChId, buf, bpm, panLaw)`. Plus orphaned engine buffers removed: `mLayerEngineSum`/`mLayerEngineScratch`/`mBassEngineBuf`/`mBassEngineScratch` (+ prepare-time sizing + the dispatch-time `mLayerEngineSum` size/clear + orphaned `numRenderCh`); dead since the serial-tail deletion; same family as the `mChannelAccum` removed in Task 2.

- **Six bugs surfaced + fixed in-batch** (per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md` — Jeff's directive 2026-05-22: "bugs surfaced *by* this cleanup get fixed in-batch, not handed to a follow-up; the third deferral-lean correction this batch — internalized: do the complete job, fix what's found"):

  **(a) Project-load crash on the single MT path** — during verify (Multi-core Rendering OFF, single-core diagnostic) Jeff loaded a save file and hit a crash. Stack: `RenderGraphDispatcher::dispatchBlock:239` (leaf-seed loop deref of `t->mInitialDeps`) <- `VibeSynthProcessor::processBlock` <- WASAPI audio thread, on project load. **Owned as exposed-by-QA-Ef, not introduced** (per `feedback_own_the_codebase_no_git_alibi.md`): a latent rebuild race that QA-Ef unmasked. Before QA-Ef, single-core diagnostic mode ran a separate serial path that never called `dispatchBlock` during a block, so the race could not fire in that mode; QA-Ef put every mode on the one `RenderGraphDispatcher` path, so `dispatchBlock` now runs during a block in both modes and the latent race began crashing on load. **Root cause:** the project-load shield (`mProjectLoadInProgress` atomic, which makes `processBlock` bail to silence during a load) covered only the TEARDOWN half of a load — `StandaloneEditor::closeAllDynamicTabs` cleared the shield on exit. The REBUILD half (recreating the loaded project's tabs/engines/tasks) ran with the shield DOWN. During rebuild, `RenderGraphDispatcher::registerTask` did `mTasks.push_back` on a bare `std::vector<RenderTask*>` with no reserved capacity; loading a project larger than the app's current task capacity grew + relocated the vector while the audio thread was concurrently iterating `mTasks` every block (in `dispatchBlock`'s leaf-seed loop AND `rebuildLinks`) -> use-after-free / garbage pointer -> crash. **SC-loadcrash (Jeff 2026-05-22) = (C) Both** — defense in depth: the reserve is a universal safety floor, the shield is the correct lifecycle fix. **Fix:** (1) `RenderGraphDispatcher.cpp` ctor `mTasks.reserve(kMaxStripChannels + 64)` + `mSyntheticDeps.reserve(256)` so registration never reallocates under the audio thread (universal floor — covers EVERY rebuild path including load, New Project, restore-backup, live tab adds); (2) `PluginProcessor.cpp::deserializeProject` raises the shield (`setProjectLoadInProgress(true)` + `juce::Thread::sleep(30)` drain) at the top of its full body and lowers it at the end after `onDeserializeUIState` fires the UI rebuild — so the heavy tab/engine rebuild runs shielded; (3) `StandaloneEditor.cpp::closeAllDynamicTabs` made nest-aware — saves the prior shield state and only drains/clears when it is the OUTERMOST owner, so when wrapped by `deserializeProject` it leaves the shield raised through the rebuild; (4) `StandaloneEditor.cpp::restoreAudioStripsFromArrangement` shields the audio-row rebuild (`ensureAudioInsert` -> `registerTask` + `applyPendingRackStates`) when `isLoadContext`, also nest-aware. Plus a doc-comment update on `mProjectLoadInProgress` in `PluginProcessor.h` documenting that the shield must wrap the full load (teardown + rebuild), not just teardown.

  **(b) Shield scope extension to New Project + apply-template** — Jeff caught my dismissal of the blank-New rebuild as "crash-safe via reserve, fresh project has no audio playing." Wrong on both counts: File > New can be hit while a prior project is open AND playing; the audio device calls `processBlock` -> `dispatchBlock` -> iterates the task list every block regardless of transport state. So New Project + apply-template ARE load-type rebuilds that touch the graph while audio is live. The Task-0 reserve already prevented the realloc so New-while-playing wouldn't have crashed even unshielded (x86 TSO + reserve); the gap was a brief partial-graph render (possible blip), not a crash. **SC-loadcrash-scope (Jeff 2026-05-22) = (A) shield all load-type rebuilds.** **Fix:** `StandaloneEditor.cpp::loadTemplate` got a nest-aware shield wrap of its full body (covers the standalone "Load Template" menu caller; nest-inherits when `doFileNew` already raised it); `StandaloneEditor.cpp::doFileNew` got a nest-aware shield wrap of the rebuild section (the blank-New `addDefaultDynamicTabs` path + the `loadTemplate` call). Intentionally left unshielded by design (reserve-protected): direct kit load, live single-tab adds, editor-ctor default tabs (`:1551`, fresh start / no prior audio). Shielding those would drop audio on a mid-session edit.

  **(c) Aux cable persistence — `serializeProject` was missing nodes for lazily-registered params** — `serializeProject` saves via `apvts.copyState()`. Lazily-registered mixer-strip params (aux added mid-session via "Add Mixer Strip") get a `RangedAudioParameter` but NO node in `apvts.state` until a `replaceState` rebind runs (`updateParameterConnectionsToChildTrees` appends a node for any adapter lacking one; triggered by `replaceState`'s `state = newState` -> `valueTreeRedirected`). That rebind only fires on load / New Project, so an aux added AFTER the last rebind was invisible to `copyState()` -> never saved -> nothing for `restoreAuxStripsFromState` to find on reload. Diagnosed via Save Diag instrumentation (temp `serializeProject` logging — `mixer_layers_send0_to` reported live = 100 BEFORE rebind, = -1 AFTER, written = -1 on first save; 100/100/100 on second save). **First fix attempt** ran `apvts.replaceState(apvts.copyState())` before snapshotting — but the rebind created an EMPTY `<PARAM>` node (id only, no `value` property) at the line-442 `appendChild`, which fired `valueTreeChildAdded` -> `setNewState` -> read the new node's missing-value default and called `setDenormalisedValue(default)` -> live param RESET to default; for `mixer_layers_send0_to` the default is -1, so the cable's send was silently nulled on the first save after drawing the cable. **Fix v2 (final):** replaced the destructive rebind with manual node creation in `serializeProject` — iterate `getParameters()`, and for each `RangedAudioParameter` that lacks a node in `apvts.state`, append a `<PARAM>` child with its CURRENT live value **pre-set**. The same `valueTreeChildAdded` listener fires but reads the correct value -> `setDenormalisedValue` is a no-op -> param keeps its current value. Verified: cable persists on the FIRST reload after a save. The Save Diag instrumentation was stripped before close (grep clean).

  **(d) Aux strips leaking across project loads** — `mAuxInserts` + `mAuxRenderTasks` had no teardown hook; `ensureAuxInsert`'s comment literally said "auxes persist for the project lifetime." Open 16 auxes -> load another project -> all 16 remain. **Spec call (Jeff 2026-05-22):** tear down aux engine state on project load. **First fix attempt** added `mProcessor.clearAllAuxInserts()` to `StandaloneEditor::closeAllDynamicTabs` — broke restore on open-project because `deserializeUIState` calls `closeAllDynamicTabs` AGAIN (nested, at :9685) AFTER `restoreAuxStripsFromState` had already rebuilt the aux nodes in `deserializeProject`; the nested call wiped the just-restored aux state. **Refactor:** moved `clearAllAuxInserts()` out of `closeAllDynamicTabs` entirely and into the three load-entry points, each already under its own load shield (so audio-thread mutation of the render task list is safe): `VibeSynthProcessor::deserializeProject` (right after shield-set + drain, before `replaceState`); `StandaloneEditor::doFileNew` (after shield-set + drain); `StandaloneEditor::loadTemplate` (after shield-set + drain). Inner `closeAllDynamicTabs` inside `deserializeUIState` no longer touches aux. **Second bug found mid-verify — phantom rebind nodes:** AT1 (3 auxes) -> AT2 (1 aux) showed all 3 still. Root cause: `apvts.replaceState` triggers JUCE's rebind, which appends EMPTY `<PARAM>` nodes for adapters that lack a tree child (any param registered in a prior session but absent from this file); on AT1->AT2, `mixer_aux_1/2` are still registered `RangedAudioParameter`s (`createAndAddParameter` has no inverse) so the rebind creates phantom empty nodes for them; `restoreAuxStripsFromState`'s scan of `apvts.state` for `mixer_aux_N_level` ids then saw the real `mixer_aux_0` from AT2 + the phantom `mixer_aux_1/2` -> called `ensureAuxInsert` for all three -> phantom auxes recreated. **Fix:** changed `restoreAuxStripsFromState` to take `const juce::ValueTree& sourceState`; callers (`setStateInformation` + `deserializeProject`) take a `state.createCopy()` deep snapshot BEFORE `replaceState` and pass it in (deep copy because `replaceState` does `apvts.state = newState` (shared underlying), so a non-copied reference would be mutated by the rebind too). New `VibeGraph::clearAuxInserts()` clears `mAuxInserts`; new `PluginProcessor::clearAllAuxInserts()` unregisters tasks + clears `mAuxRenderTasks` + calls `VibeGraph::clearAuxInserts()`. Verified: AT1 (3 auxes) -> AT2 (1 aux) -> shows only 1; fresh-launch + open AT2 shows 1; File > New + add auxes -> open another project -> clean.

  **(e) Adding an aux didn't flag dirty** — `MixerPage::addAuxChannel()` is the user entry point (both the "Add Aux Strip" button click and the cable-spawn-new-aux path route through it; load path uses `addAuxChannelAtIndex` directly and bypasses). Added `if (mProcessor.onAnyStateChange) mProcessor.onAnyStateChange();` to `addAuxChannel()`. `markDirty` itself respects `ProjectManager::mIgnoreDirty` as a belt-and-suspenders. **Second bug surfaced by verify — Untitled title suppressed the dirty marker:** fresh app launch + adding an aux showed no dirty marker. Root cause: `StandaloneEditor::refreshWindowTitle` only added the " *" suffix INSIDE the `if (mProjectManager->hasProject())` branch — on a fresh launch with no project folder set yet, `hasProject()` returns false, so the title bar conditionally suppressed the marker even though `markDirty` had correctly set `mDirty=true` and `onDirtyChanged` had fired `refreshWindowTitle`. **Fix:** `refreshWindowTitle` now shows `BaySickDAW - <project name>` if a project is set OR `BaySickDAW - Untitled` if none, and appends ` *` whenever `isDirty()` regardless. Fresh-launch + edit now reads `BaySickDAW - Untitled *`.

  **(f) File > New wasn't blank** — auto-applied default template which then partial-loaded with broken Drums on the load side (tab name missing, content empty except for `+ New Drum` / `+ Rusty`). **Misdiagnosis owned** (per Jeff's "describe what code does without asking 'is this even right'" pattern feedback): I initially claimed File > New was loading the user's default template; Jeff explicitly corrected ("it's not the default template loading - it's a blank template with 1 layer + 1 bass with engines pre-selected and locked"). Reconciliation: Test Kit DOES load via `loadTemplate(getDefaultTemplate())` in `doFileNew`, but its kit drums come back broken on the load side - so Jeff was seeing partial-load: Layer + Bass restored, Drums broken. The "pre-selected and locked" was the natural "lock after first engine pick" behavior fired by `loadTemplate` restoring the engine choice. **Spec call:** File > New always loads blank, identical to the editor ctor's first-launch state; default-template auto-application moves to a future "New from Default Template" menu item (deferred to QA-ProjectSave). **Fix:** removed `const auto tpl = mProjectManager->getDefaultTemplate();`, the `if (tpl.existsAsFile() ...) loadTemplate(tpl)` block, and the entire `needDefaults` check + loop from `doFileNew`. The function now unconditionally calls `addDefaultDynamicTabs()` -> `mRibbon->selectTab(3)` -> `onTabSelected(3)`, matching the editor ctor's invocation. The default-template `settings.xml` entry remains and is still surfaced under Options > Set Default Template; it just no longer auto-applies on plain File > New.

- **Side finding flagged + interim landed (not the surviving value of the batch) — FX-bus meter dead under MT:** found during the VibeGraph-half verify. The FX bus carries its peak on `EffectsBusNode` (like L/B/D/Master) but is absent from Group-1 node-drains; its Group-2 `mFxBusPeakDb*Run` mirrors were populated only by the serial tail (`drainEffectsBusPeakDbStereo()` + CAS-max) -> serial-only -> dead under MT all along (i.e., the FX bus had been silently mis-metered since MT became the production default). **Interim Group-2-style fix landed:** `drainMeterAtomicsForUI` now drains `drainEffectsBusPeakDbStereo()` + CAS-max into the Run mirrors so the existing Run -> snapshot promotion feeds the meter. **Full unification routed to QA-Eg** (see §9 twenty-eighth Forks + the routing table below): the FX-meter diagnosis surfaced the deeper architectural finding that bus peak metering uses TWO mechanisms with no deliberate decision behind the split (G1 = node-owned, used by L/B/D/Master; G2 = centralized mirror, used by Clips/Vox/Inst/Rusty/FX). Jeff explicitly flagged the underlying G1-vs-G2 split as needing its own batch ("the split is arbitrary as I never asked for this, you made one and then when you started adding stuff you made another and never confirmed with me which of these two should be implemented or which one I want to use"). Routing call (per Jeff's "fix small in-batch / plan big to its own batch", 2026-05-22): this is the "big" kind -> new dedicated batch, NOT folded into QA-Ef (which would have re-churned the in-flight verify). QA-Ef's interim FX-bus meter fix stays as the transitional G2-style piece; QA-Eg supersedes it.

- **Three batches routed at close** (slots SURFACED to Jeff per `feedback_slot_placement_is_spec_call.md`):
  - **QA-Eg — bus-meter draining unification (G1 standardization)** — see §9 twenty-eighth Forks entry. Slotted **immediately after QA-Ef, before QA-Ed.** Standardize on G1 (node-owned atomics + `drainBusPeakDbStereo()` accessors); migrate Clips/Vox/Inst/Rusty + FX off the centralized `PluginProcessor` running-max mirror; rewrite `drainMeterAtomicsForUI`; delete the QA-Ef interim FX-bus drain. Risk low-medium, effort ~4-7 hours.

Pure UX swap, independent of templates/samples/project-save. Risk low-medium, effort ~4-7 hours.
  - **QA-ProjectSave — project save / template / sample handling (consolidated)** — see §9 thirtieth Forks entry. Slotted **at the end of the Phase 1-5 chain, after QA-Export** (Jeff: "must come AFTER anything that could affect saves or add things that should be saved, so we capture everything"). Absorbs the full #7 deferral (template scope expansion to save vox/inst/clip/rusty/aux/samples; `loadTemplate` non-destructive teardown; drum inline-load fix; New-from-Template submenu restructure replacing menu items 102 + 109; unified Load Template dirty-check flow; removals of `doFileNewFromTemplate` + `showTemplateMenu` + duplicate `kIdSaveAs`) plus the sample-retention / FL-Studio-style file handling discussion (source-aware hybrid: Factory + user library = reference, volatile drops = copy; explicit "Pack project" action; migration story for existing per-project-copy samples; UI indicators). Risk high (touches project XML save/load + template format + audio-library state + sample-path resolution + three menu surfaces), effort ~12-18 hours.

- **Close — `/review-batch QA-Ef` (hot-path rip-out, mandatory):** READY-TO-COMMIT after in-batch fixes. **0 BLOCKERs, 4 NEEDS-FIX, 5 NITs.** All 4 NEEDS-FIX **fixed in-batch** per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md`: (1) **24-site serial-ghost comment sweep** across `Source/` — 14 reviewer-flagged sites still describing the deleted serial path / `routeInsertOutput` / dual-path framing as if live, PLUS 10 stale `kEnableMultiThreadedEngine` (defunct constexpr) references swept (scope-expanded once I grep-confirmed the same class of finding); the close keeps 3 explicit historical markers ("now-deleted", "now-stale") as durable carry-forward record. (2) **3 `clearAllAuxInserts` doc-comments retargeted** to point to the three load-entry points (`deserializeProject` / `doFileNew` / `loadTemplate`) instead of the abandoned first-iteration `closeAllDynamicTabs` placement. (3) **Host-callback parity** — `setStateInformation` got shield + drain + `clearAllAuxInserts()` mirroring `deserializeProject`; Jeff overruled my initial "comment-only because the path is dead in standalone" lean ("what is necessary though?") and I scope-expanded to also patch `getStateInformation` with the manual-node-creation block mirroring `serializeProject` so the VST3 host save path inherits the same correctness floor as standalone Save. (4) **AlertWindow label** `MT mode: ON/OFF` -> `Multi-core: ON / OFF (single-core diagnostic)` to match the user-facing menu wording post-QA-Ef (toggle parks workers, doesn't switch paths). 5 NITs deferred per pre-existing-or-stylistic threshold: (i) vestigial empty `{ }` + unreachable `return;` at `PluginProcessor.cpp:1839/1878-1881` (documented by adjacent comment); (ii) truncated section divider at `VibeGraph.cpp:2998` post-`processAuxInserts` removal; (iii) two 30 ms `juce::Thread::sleep(30)` drains per Open Project flow (`deserializeProject` + `restoreAudioStripsFromArrangement` each raise own shield) — possible future unification under one outer shield API; (iv) UTF-8 BOM stripped from `PluginProcessor.cpp:1` (innocuous, matches rest of `Source/`); (v) untracked `Templates/My Templates/` (Jeff's local verify test asset, out-of-scope, NOT staged per `feedback_surface_full_git_status_before_commit.md`). #### Found along the way

- **FND-1 (project-load crash on the single MT path)** — latent rebuild race unmasked by deleting the serial path. Fixed in-batch (a). SC-loadcrash = (C) Both.
- **FND-2 (shield-scope dismissal on New Project + apply-template)** — I'd filed the blank New Project rebuild as "crash-safe via reserve, fresh project has no audio playing." Jeff caught both errors (File > New can be hit while audio is live; `dispatchBlock` runs every block regardless of transport state). Fixed in-batch (b). SC-loadcrash-scope = (A) shield all load-type rebuilds. **Owned per `feedback_own_the_codebase_no_git_alibi.md`.**
- **FND-3 (aux cable persistence)** — first diagnosis was wrong (I'd asserted "save works, the bug is load-side, every link reads correct" - but I'd checked a DIFFERENT save file and never traced the teardown). Invoked `superpowers:systematic-debugging` and gathered evidence: the bug is save-side, lazily-registered params never made it into the saved XML. First fix attempt (rebind-before-snapshot) reset the live param to default via the JUCE `appendChild` -> `valueTreeChildAdded` -> `setNewState` chain. Fix v2 (manual node creation with current live value pre-set) worked. Owned per `feedback_own_the_codebase_no_git_alibi.md` + `feedback_check_code_before_calling_it_expected.md`.
- **FND-4 (aux strips leaking across project loads)** — first fix attempt (clear in `closeAllDynamicTabs`) broke restore on open-project because `deserializeUIState` nests `closeAllDynamicTabs` AFTER restore. Refactored to call from the three load-entry points each under their own shield. Second bug surfaced mid-verify (phantom rebind nodes on AT1->AT2) — fixed via deep-copy snapshot before `replaceState`. Both rounds in-batch.
- **FND-5 (Untitled title suppressed the dirty marker)** — surfaced by the aux-dirty fix verify. `refreshWindowTitle` only appended " *" inside `hasProject()`. Fixed in-batch (e) follow-up.
- **FND-6 (File > New misdiagnosis)** — I initially claimed File > New was loading the user's default template; Jeff explicitly corrected ("it's not the default template loading - it's a blank template with 1 layer + 1 bass with engines pre-selected and locked"). Reconciliation: Test Kit DOES load via `loadTemplate(getDefaultTemplate())` in `doFileNew`, but its kit drums come back broken on the load side; the "pre-selected and locked" was the natural lock-after-first-pick. Owned. Fixed in-batch (f).
- **FND-7 (template scope is incomplete + `loadTemplate` is destructive + drum inline-load skips inline children)** — scoping investigation of original #7 (template menu work) revealed `saveTemplateAs` saves only L/B/D (no vox/inst/clip/rusty/aux/samples) AND `loadTemplate`'s `closeAllDynamicTabs()` destroys vox/inst/clip/rusty tabs in the destination project before restoring only L/B/D. I initially framed the choice as a 3-option spec call; Jeff overruled the framing ("the L/B/D-only template scope was fundamentally incomplete for what 'template' means, the destructive teardown is functional brokenness (not a tradeoff)"). Pattern feedback owned: "this is yet another perfect example of you not following up on all the places things need to be updated." Routed to QA-ProjectSave (consolidated batch absorbing the full #7 deferral + sample retention).
- **FND-8 (native OS file dialogs)** — Jeff's testing during QA-Ef surfaced the issue ("the button opens a windows style file opener... we don't use this style of window for opening files and instead have these kind of old and clunky looking internal windows that pop up... never asked about this nor would I want that"). Jeff explicitly routed it: "can be addressed in a separate batch." Routed to QA-NativeDialogs.
- **FND-9 (bus-meter draining is two ad-hoc mechanisms - G1 vs G2)** — surfaced by the FX-bus meter fix. I (codebase author) introduced G1, then G2 when later buses were added, and never surfaced "which do we standardize on" as a spec call — unilateral architectural choice. Jeff overruled the split ("the split is arbitrary as I never asked for this... never confirmed with me which of these two should be implemented or which one I want to use"). Routed to QA-Eg.
- **Process misses owned this batch:** (1) **three deferral-leans corrected** — twice on the core rip-out task (Option A "dead but harmless" leftovers + Option 2 "comments only, defer the deeper infrastructure" — both overruled by Jeff to "leave no serial ghost" full removal) and once on the aux fixes (initially leaned to route the aux bugs to a follow-up; Jeff: "bugs surfaced *by* this cleanup get fixed in-batch"). Internalized — do the complete job. (2) **drafter-firing cadence wrong** — fired `/draft-doc running-notes` (and even wrote entries directly) too often during execution earlier in QA-Ef; correct cadence is one drafter dispatch per completed task, applied once verified. (3) **plan-vs-do framing on incomplete-by-design code** — recurring pattern Jeff called out twice this turn (the L/B/D-only template scope framing; the option-1/2 framing of the destructive teardown): I describe what code does and stop there instead of asking "is this even right" or "does this affect everywhere it should." Internalize: when the analysis surfaces an incomplete-by-design state, FLAG it as a problem rather than presenting it as a viable option. (4) **misdiagnosis on aux persistence (Bug A)** — overclaimed before checking the actual save file Jeff opened. (5) **misdiagnosis on File > New (#6)** — described code behavior without verifying it matched the reported symptom.

#### Carry-forward contradictions (if any)

- **Carry-Forward Reference §1 (MT Render Path Primitives) - the "No dead wiring" rule retires for the render engine.** The carry-forward locked the rule that no dead wiring is allowed in the render engine because the ST/MT flag gated two paths. With the serial path deleted and the flag stopping gating two paths (it now only gates worker-park vs full-parallel within the single dispatcher), the Composite RenderTask is the only summation path; the rule is moot for the render engine. Noted here for future readers.
- **Carry-Forward §4 (DSP-12 = Composite RenderTask) - confirmed registered + survives.** No contradiction; Task 1 verified `CompositeAudioInsertTask` is registered at `:4765`, so deleting the serial summation did not re-expose DSP-12.
- **§9 twenty-fifth Forks "mirrored / inert / ST-only" inventory line numbers were drifted** - the §9 inventory was captured against pre-QA-Ea source; Task 1 re-measured the exact bounds against current post-`c648fb7`/`f28319e`/`c5c5deb` source (serial tail `:2008-2837`; MT branch `:1931`; early return `:2005`). Inventory itself was correct in substance; only the line numbers needed refresh.
- **`mProjectLoadInProgress` shield doc-comment was incomplete** - pre-QA-Ef comment described the shield as covering "the load" without distinguishing teardown vs rebuild. The latent rebuild race (FND-1) was masked by the dual-path setup (single-core diagnostic ran a separate serial path that didn't call `dispatchBlock`). Doc-comment updated to document that the shield must wrap the full load (teardown + rebuild), not just teardown. Recorded here for the durable record.
- **`ensureAuxInsert` comment was wrong** - literally said "auxes persist for the project lifetime," which the project-load-leak bug (FND-4) proved was a missing-teardown bug, not a design intent. Comment removed; teardown wired from the three load-entry points.

#### Done

QA-Eg lifted every G2 bus off the centralized `PluginProcessor` running-max mirror (the `*Run` indirection populated by `processBus` CAS-max + drained via promote-to-snapshot) onto the same G1 pattern Layers / Bass / Drums / Master already used (node-internal `peakDb / peakDbL / peakDbR` atomics exchange-stored into `VibeGraph` public-member atomics, drained directly via the G1 loop in `drainMeterAtomicsForUI`).  The 8 G2 buses (**FX, AudioClips, Vox, Vox2, Inst, Inst2, Inst3, RustyDrums**) migrated one at a time across Tasks 2-6, each individually owner-verified, with the structural extension of `InstrChannelNode` (the 7 InstrChannelNode-backed buses' shared substrate) landing one-time in Task 3.  Task 7 ran the natural post-migration cleanup (deleting the now-dead G2 infrastructure entirely) plus absorbed a mid-batch Mixer UX bundle Jeff requested and closed the actual idle-state "dying lightbulb" cable flicker that surfaced during owner verification of the new cable rendering.  Six commits over the source-touching tasks plus one open commit and one close commit; QA-Ef's interim FX-bus Group-2-style fix superseded as part of the unification.  The net architectural change is that all 11 buses (L / B / D / Master + the 8 migrated G2 buses + the pre-existing FX path that QA-Ef interim-fixed) now publish through the identical end-to-end path with no centralized mirror layer between bus nodes and the UI snapshot.

- **Task 0 — open commit (docs only)** (`6594b3a`): plan file `Plans & Specs/Batch Plans/squishy-scribbling-flurry.md` mirrored from `~/.claude/plans/` + home-dir copy deleted (per `feedback_plan_mirror_one_way.md`); §5 QA-Eg `**Plan file:**` pointer flipped from the placeholder `<silly-name>.md (when started)` form to the backticked path; running-notes seed `Plans & Specs/Running Notes/squishy-scribbling-flurry.md` created per §0 required-sections + Diagnostic Instrumentation Catalog stub.  Five spec calls locked with Jeff 2026-05-23: **S1 = all 8 G2 buses** (Clips / FX / Vox / Inst / Rusty + Vox2 / Inst2 / Inst3 — leaving secondaries on `*Run` re-creates exactly the split this batch removes); **S2 = per-row Builder audio meters DEFERRED** to a new dedicated batch (same architectural smell confirmed by source-read at `PluginProcessor.h:645-654 + :620-622 + CompositeAudioInsertTask.cpp:113-115`; folding would inflate QA-Eg scope + cross test surfaces); **S3 = per-bus tasks** (FX → Clips → Vox+Vox2 → Inst+Inst2+Inst3 → Rusty → cleanup — each task individually verifiable by ear; clean rollback boundaries; FX first because `EffectsBusNode` was already G1-shaped — smallest scope to validate the pattern); **S4 = `publishPeakReading()` with the per-block latency-comp ring buffer** (not the simpler CAS-max — whole point of standardizing is uniformity across all 11 buses); **S5 = silly-name `squishy-scribbling-flurry`** (drafter's pick per `feedback_silly_name_is_my_pick.md`).

- **Task 1 — pre-flight inventory (read-only)** + S6 side finding surfaced & resolved: direct reads of all 5 G1 BusNode struct definitions ([Source/VibeGraph.cpp:217 / :420 / :587 / :752 / :901](../Source/VibeGraph.cpp)) confirmed the uniform peak field set (`peakDb / peakDbL / peakDbR` atomics + `peakRingL / peakRingR` arrays + `peakRingIdx`) — Task 3's `InstrChannelNode` extension copies this exact pattern, no invention.  The universal publish helper `publishPeakReading` ([VibeGraph.cpp:91](../Source/VibeGraph.cpp)) has 5 G1 call-sites at `:413, :580, :743, :885, :1038`; Tasks 3-6 add the same call to `processBus`'s generic-bus section pointing at the new `InstrChannelNode` fields.  Cleanup infrastructure confirmed self-contained: `registerBusPeakAtomics` has 7 callers (all in PluginProcessor.cpp); `mBusPeakRefs` used ONLY by the registration + the CAS-max site; `drainEffectsBusPeakDbStereo` has exactly 1 caller (the QA-Ef interim block that Task 2 deletes); no consumers outside PluginProcessor + VibeGraph — Task 7's wholesale delete is safe.  **Side finding S6 (resolved same task):** `peakDecayDbPerBlock` is dead state on every G1 BusNode — initialized + recalculated in `prepare()` (10 sites across the 5 nodes) but never read; dead carry-over from the pre-2026-05-02 meter-ballistics model that the lock-free `publishPeakReading` rewrite obsoleted.  Jeff's call: skip the field on the new InstrChannelNode AND delete it + its prepare-time recalc lines from the 5 existing G1 BusNodes as part of Task 7 cleanup — net post-batch, all 11 buses uniform with no dead carry-over.  No source touched this task; no commit.

- **Task 2 — FX bus migration to G1** (`0b33ffe`): added `fxBusPeakDb / fxBusPeakDbL / fxBusPeakDbR` `VibeGraph` public-member atomics; rewrote `kFxBus` dispatch in `processBus` from single-line into multi-line block with 3 exchange-stores parallel to L/B/D; moved `constexpr float kBusNegInf` declaration up so FX shares the same scope; added 3 `drainAndMerge` lines to the G1 loop in `drainMeterAtomicsForUI` + DELETED the entire QA-Ef interim FX-bus drain block + updated Group 2 comment to remove "FxBus" mention; deleted `mFxBusPeakDbRun / *L / *R` declarations from PluginProcessor.h. - **Task 7 — Mixer page UX scope-add + idle-cable-flicker fix + bus-peak indirection dead-code sweep** (`1942ae2`): the close commit, three orthogonal pieces in one task.  **(i) Bus-peak indirection dead-code sweep** (the natural post-migration cleanup): `VibeGraph::BusPeakRefs` struct + `mBusPeakRefs` table + `registerBusPeakAtomics()` API removed entirely; `processBus`'s G2 CAS-max fallback branch (the else-arm that ran when node was nullptr) deleted; `getEffectsBusPeakDbStereo()` + `drainEffectsBusPeakDbStereo()` removed (Master/FX now drain through the same G1 loop as every other bus); the per-bus `peakDecayDbPerBlock` field + its `prepareToPlay` computation removed from all five L/B/D/Master/FX BusNode types (the running decay was a leftover from an earlier metering scheme superseded by the FL-parity `DBFSMeter` ballistic filter that now lives in SharedUI); the corresponding `prepareToPlay` registration comment block in `PluginProcessor::prepareToPlay` deleted.  Net post-batch: all 11 buses uniform; no dead state across any node type; G2 mirror state fully gone from `PluginProcessor.h/.cpp` (grep clean).  **(ii) Mixer page UX bundle** (mid-batch request Jeff layered onto QA-Eg): mixer scrollbar relocated from top to bottom with all strips raised by the saved space and Master strip extended to full page height; cable shape redesigned as a deep-tension dual-stub Bezier (200 px base stub length + 0.4 distance multiplier so the cable's middle dangles below the visible page area) applied through a new `CableOverlay::getMixerCablePath` helper so every cable + ghost-cable site shares the same math; per-cable alpha + warning-color modulation driven by the source strip's current displayed peak via a new `cableTelemetry` lambda (dB-normalized -60..0 mapping, warning color lerps red above -0.45 dB); right-click hit-test on a spot with multiple overlapping cables now opens a `PopupMenu` chooser via the new `hitTestCablesAll` variant (mains entries appear greyed/disabled — drag to reroute, no action callout; only sends and sidechain entries open the action callout); `CableMainOutPopup` class deleted entirely (the "glorified tooltip" main-out info popup); send slot fan-out re-anchored so the first send lands exactly at the socket dot (`{0, 8, -8, 16}`); hot-pink `#ff33b0` send cable for perceptual brightness parity with green main; `DBFSMeter` gains delta-time ballistic smoothing (20 ms attack constant, exponential filter) so meter LEDs and cable telemetry stay visually in sync and don't pop on transients; `MixerTrackStrip` exposes `getCurrentPeakDb()` forwarding to `mMeter.getCurrentDisplayedDb()` (also new) so the cable telemetry reads the same smoothed value the meter LEDs render — no separate smoothing layer, no double-smoothing artefacts.  **(iii) Idle-cable-flicker fix** (the actual problem that surfaced during owner verification of the new cable rendering): `MixerPage::onVBlank` was unconditionally calling `mCableOverlay->repaint()` every vblank, and because the overlay is transparent each paint clears the cables and redraws on transparent background — even when telemetry alpha/color didn't change between frames, the clear-then-redraw timing momentarily exposed the underlying background between clear and new draw, which is the "dying lightbulb" Jeff was seeing.  Diagnostic capture (3 `[CABLE-DIAG]` sites + a `cableDiagLog()` helper writing direct `juce::FileOutputStream` appends to `Documents/BaySickDAW/cable_diag.log`, since `juce::Logger::writeToLog` was a static routing to a nullptr global logger that silently dropped the first attempt) confirmed `paint()` firing at ~7/s on idle with ZERO alpha-change events, proving the data was stable and the issue lived purely in the rendering layer.  Fix gates the repaint on a per-frame snapshot of every strip's `getCurrentPeakDb()` (new `mLastPeakSnapshot` vector member in `MixerPage.h`) and only repaints `CableOverlay` when at least one strip's displayed peak changed by > 0.1 dB since the previous vblank; the snapshot iteration covers all bus strips (Master / Layers / Bass / Drums / FX / AudioClips / Vox / Inst / Rusty / Vox2 / Inst2 / Inst3) plus every insert strip map (Layer / Bass / Drum / Audio / Aux / Vox / Inst / Rusty), with `-inf` comparisons correctly yielding NaN which fails `NaN > 0.1` so no repaint at the floor, vector-size mismatch from strip add/remove triggering immediate repaint, and `-inf` <-> finite transitions yielding `inf` delta which correctly repaints.  Diagnostic instrumentation walked: `cableDiagLog` helper + the 3 `[CABLE-DIAG]` sites removed per the Diagnostic Instrumentation Catalog disposition column.

- **Task 8 — close sequence**: `/draft-doc batch-close` produced the initial entry; `/review-batch QA-Eg` flagged **5 NEEDS-FIX + 7 NITs** — all fixed in-batch per `feedback_closed_batch_carryforward_via_forks.md` (real bugs surfaced mid-QA-batch get fixed in-batch by default per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md`); `/perf-audit` flagged **8 findings** — **3 folded into Task 8** (H2 `findStripByChannelId` unordered_map cache replacing the 11-bus + 8-map linear scan; M1 `onVBlank` scratch+swap vector reuse eliminating the 60Hz heap alloc; H4 `EffectRack` peak-loop SIMD via `juce::FloatVectorOperations::findMinAndMax`), **3 routed forward** as a perf-audit cluster of new dedicated batches (H1 = QA-InsertMaps, H3 = QA-VoicePool, M2 = QA-EngineApvts), **2 minor** (M3 absorbed by QA-InsertMaps; L1 deferred as low-priority).  **Plus FND-16 — Master cable cutout / cable-over-Master visual bug** surfaced during owner-verification of the fix-up build (cables bleeding over Master strip + dimming as source strips scrolled out of viewport + right-click chooser missing cables whose sources had scrolled away); fixed in-batch via a rectangle clip-mask carved out of Master at the destination-socket position (Phase B of `CableOverlay::paint`), sentinel-only skip in `renderMasterStub` so off-screen-source cables still render through the cutout, and a Master-cutout pre-pass in `hitTestCablesAll` so the right-click chooser lists every routing-to-Master regardless of source scroll position; cutout dimensions tuned via a transient "Tune Master Cutout" calibration UI (mirroring the Rusty kit hitbox editor — drag/resize handles + Save Layout log to `Documents/BaySickDAW/mixer_master_cutout.txt`) and baked in as file-scope constants (`kMasterCutoutW=5.0` / `kMasterCutoutH=14.0` / `kMasterCutoutXOff=0.5` / `kMasterCutoutYOff=5.0`) before the calibration UI was stripped per Jeff's request once tuning was complete.  Task 8 single combined commit lands the `/review-batch` 5 NEEDS-FIX + 7 NITs fixes + the `/perf-audit` H2 + M1 + H4 fold-in + the FND-16 cable cutout fix + the close paperwork (§5 STATUS banner update + 3 new §5 entries for the cluster + §6 arrow update + §6 footnotes 20/21/22 + §9 thirty-third through thirty-fifth Forks entries + this Work Log entry expansion + running-notes Task 8 close-pass section).

  - **QA-AudioMeters — per-row Builder audio meters G1 migration** (S2 deferral from Task 1) — same dual-mirror G2 architecture as the 8 buses QA-Eg just migrated, confirmed by source-read at `PluginProcessor.h:645-654 + :620-622 + CompositeAudioInsertTask.cpp:113-115`. See §9 thirty-first Forks entry + new §5 batch row.  Risk low-medium, effort ~3-5 hours.
  - **QA-DirtyFlag — UndoManager-aware project dirty tracking** (surfaced 2026-05-23 mid-Task-3 testing) — clicking a solo button and unclicking it marks the project dirty even though the net state matches the saved file.  Root cause: `ApvtsDirtyTracker` ([Source/Standalone/ApvtsDirtyTracker.h:39-42](../Source/Standalone/ApvtsDirtyTracker.h)) is a `ValueTree::Listener` that fires `onAny` on every property write regardless of old-vs-new equality; `ProjectManager::markDirty` ([Source/ProjectManager.cpp:98-102](../Source/ProjectManager.cpp)) sets `mDirty=true` unconditionally.  The flag tracks "anything touched since load" — NOT "state differs from file."  Jeff's verbatim spec (UndoManager transaction-pointer system mimicking major DAWs) lives in running notes lines 329-369 and is copied verbatim into the §5 QA-DirtyFlag entry.  Slotted **after QA-ProjectSave** (the new final Phase 5 batch).  Slot rationale (Jeff): orthogonal to QA-ProjectSave (DirtyFlag is a control-flow change — UndoManager-plumbing audit; ProjectSave is a data-layer change — XML format + sample retention); DirtyFlag-after-ProjectSave means its codebase-wide audit naturally covers ProjectSave's new save/load code.  See §9 thirty-second Forks entry + new §5 batch row.  Risk medium-high (every `ValueTree::setProperty` call site touched), effort large (codebase-wide audit).

Dead carry-over from pre-2026-05-02 meter ballistics.  Jeff's call: skip on new InstrChannelNode + delete from existing 5 nodes in Task 7 cleanup.

Routed to a new dedicated batch (`QA-AudioMeters`) — folding would have inflated QA-Eg scope to `kMaxAudioRows` rows + the CompositeAudioInsertTask test surface.

Jeff overruled ("No like this isn't at all how this app functions" + "The simplest is for you to do your job. Look at how it actually works and tell me what I need to test").  New memory `feedback_verify_scenarios_read_app_first.md` saved — every verify rig comes from source reads, not a generic DAW mental model. Two memory updates: `feedback_verify_scenarios_read_app_first.md` extended with the partial-read-trap refinement ("read the app code" means trace the full audio + UI flow, NOT grep one symbol and infer; buses backed by live input behave differently from pattern-playback / Builder-drop buses) + new project memory `project_vox_inst_accept_prerecorded_audio.md` (Vox / Inst pages accept prerecorded audio playback as an audio source, not just live mic / line-in / sfizz engines — Jeff's natural daily test rig for those buses).
- **FND-4 (Task 5 in-task build break — same-commit fix)** — initial Task 5 edit deleted all 9 `*Run` declarations from PluginProcessor.h but the G2-promotion-removal edit only caught 3 of 9 InstBus lines, leaving 6 stale `mInstBus(2/3)PeakDb*Run` references at `:2112-2117` that failed `error C2065: undeclared identifier` in both Release + Debug.  Owned per `feedback_own_the_codebase_no_git_alibi.md`; same-commit Edit deleted the 6 stale promotion lines; `grep` confirmed zero remaining references; build clean on re-run.
- **FND-5 (Idle-cable-flicker — Task 7)** — surfaced during owner verification of the new cable rendering Jeff requested mid-batch.  "Dying lightbulb" intermittent flicker on the CableOverlay even with no audio playing.  Diagnostic capture confirmed `paint()` firing at ~7/s on idle with zero alpha-change events; root cause was unconditional `mCableOverlay->repaint()` on every vblank — the transparent overlay's clear-then-redraw timing momentarily exposed the underlying background between clear and new draw.  Fixed in-batch by gating the repaint on a per-frame peak-snapshot delta (>0.1 dB).
- **FND-6 (Project dirty flag tracks "touched since load" not "state differs from file")** — surfaced 2026-05-23 mid-Task-3 testing.  Clicking a solo button and unclicking it marks the project dirty even though net state matches.  Root cause traced to `ApvtsDirtyTracker` listener firing `onAny` regardless of equality + `ProjectManager::markDirty` setting `mDirty=true` unconditionally.  Routed to new dedicated batch (`QA-DirtyFlag`) with Jeff's verbatim UndoManager-transaction-pointer spec carried into §5.
- **FND-7 (`cableDiagLog()` had to bypass `juce::Logger`)** — first attempt at the cable-flicker diagnostic logging used instance-method `Logger::writeToLog` calls which silently dropped because the static routing went to a nullptr global logger in this app.  Switched to direct `juce::FileOutputStream` appends per call to `Documents/BaySickDAW/cable_diag.log` — followed the existing artifact-folder convention per `feedback_follow_existing_folder_convention.md` after I initially proposed a new `Logs/` subfolder and Jeff overruled.
- **FND-8 (`/perf-audit` H1 — InsertNode std::map<int, unique_ptr> lookup tax)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  `VibeGraph::processInsert` (`Source/VibeGraph.cpp:2337`) + `pushScArrayToStrip` (`:2889`) do 4x `std::map::find()` per insert per audio block.  On a busy session ~50 inserts at ~6 ms cadence = 30k+ map lookups/sec on the audio thread.  Red-black-tree walk + heap-scattered nodes (cache-unfriendly).  Architectural inconsistency: `RenderGraphDispatcher::mTasksByChannel` already uses a flat `std::array` indexed by ChannelId — same key space.  Routed to new dedicated batch (`QA-InsertMaps`) with Jeff's Option 2 locked (flatten to single `std::array<InsertNode*, kMaxStripChannels>`).  Estimated CPU win: ~1-3% on busy sessions.
- **FND-9 (`/perf-audit` H3 — VibeVoice::startNote per-note-on heap allocation)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  `VibeVoice::startNote` (`Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:581-583 + :607`) heap-allocates `MemoryAudioSource` + `ResamplingAudioSource` per note-on; `findRegion` (`:573`) heap-allocates a `std::vector<int> candidates`.  Audio-thread allocation on every drum hit / key press / audition.  No pool, no fat voices, no voice stealing.  Routed to new dedicated batch (`QA-VoicePool`) with Jeff's verbatim 4-section blueprint locked (pre-allocate in prepareToPlay + fat voices internal reuse + lock-free `std::atomic<bool> isActive` + voice stealing fallback with 10-20 sample fade-out).
- **FND-10 (`/perf-audit` M2 — 4 engine processors miss APVTS dirty-flag pattern)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  `HarmlessProcessor / VibePlayerProcessor / BaySickSynthProcessor / BaySickBassProcessor` call `updateFromApvts` unconditionally per block, each reading ~30-50 parameters via `apvts.getRawParameterValue(id)->load()`.  The documented pattern (`feedback_apvts_dirty_flag_pattern.md`) pairs a process-side `isIdentity()` short-circuit with a sync-side `ValueTree::Listener`-driven dirty flag; reference impl already in `PluginProcessor` (`Source/PluginProcessor.cpp:178`).  Routed to new dedicated batch (`QA-EngineApvts`).  Estimated CPU win: ~1-2% cumulative across the 4 engines on busy sessions.
- **FND-11 (`/perf-audit` H2 — `findStripByChannelId` walked O(N) per cable per paint)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  `MixerPage::findStripByChannelId` was a 19-step linear scan (11 unique_ptr bus checks + 8 `std::map` iterations) called multiple times per cable per paint (from `cableTelemetry` + `getSocketPosition` + the flash + chooser code).  Folded into Task 8 fix-up commit: added `mutable std::unordered_map<int, MixerTrackStrip*> mStripByChannelId` + `mutable bool mStripCacheDirty` to MixerPage; `findStripByChannelId` now rebuilds the cache lazily on dirty + does O(1) lookup; the cache is invalidated at 5 erase sites + clearDynamicStrips + clearAllRustyChannels + at top of `layoutScrollContent` (broad invalidation on every structural change).
- **FND-12 (`/perf-audit` M1 — `onVBlank` heap-allocates fresh std::vector every 60Hz frame)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  The peak-snapshot delta-gate landed in Task 7's idle-flicker fix built a fresh `std::vector<float> snapshot` per frame and `std::move`'d it to `mLastPeakSnapshot`, leaving the local at capacity=0 to reallocate next frame.  Folded into Task 8 fix-up commit: added `mPeakSnapshotScratch` member; onVBlank now `clear()`s scratch and `push_back`s into it, then `std::swap`s with `mLastPeakSnapshot` at the end so both vectors retain their capacity allocator-warm across frames.  Extends FND-5 from Task 7 with no UI-thread heap-alloc on the vblank path after warm-up.
- **FND-13 (`/perf-audit` H4 — `EffectRack::process` scalar peak loops, SIMD candidate)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/perf-audit` at close.  Two scalar peak loops in `EffectRack::process` (input peak at `:444-449`, output peak at `:513-518`) ran `for (int i = 0; i < numSamples; ++i) inPeak = juce::jmax(inPeak, std::abs(d[i]));` — at 256 samples × 2 channels × 6 slots × ~30 racks = 92k samples scanned per block.  Folded into Task 8 fix-up commit: swapped both loops to `juce::FloatVectorOperations::findMinAndMax(d, numSamples)` then `std::max(std::abs(range.getStart()), std::abs(range.getEnd()))` — vectorizes to SSE/AVX where available.  Estimated CPU win: ~0.5-1% at 256-sample buffer on busy session; bigger on the longer 1024-sample buffer.
- **FND-14 (`/review-batch` NEEDS-FIX bundle, 5 items)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/review-batch QA-Eg` at close.  5 NEEDS-FIX items: orphan `VibeGraph.h` comment fragment (4-line leftover from the deleted `registerBusPeakAtomics` declaration, now visually attached to the unrelated `processEffectsBus` C.1 comment); stale `drainMeterAtomicsForUI` header comment (still described the pre-batch "Three groups" model with Run mirrors that are now gone); en-GB spellings x5 ("greyed" x3 + "artefacts" x2 in QA-Eg-added code per `feedback_us_english_spelling.md` compliance); send-cable hit-zone offset bug (real functional bug — `hitTestCablesAll` used un-offset `src.x` while paint applies `kSendOffsets[idx]` — sends 2/3/4 missed their hit zones by up to 16 px); stale `mLastViewportX` comment (referenced "(now buffered-to-image) overlay" but `setBufferedToImage(true)` was removed this batch).  All fixed in Task 8 fix-up commit per `feedback_closed_batch_carryforward_via_forks.md`.
- **FND-15 (`/review-batch` NITs bundle, 7 items)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 by `/review-batch QA-Eg` at close.  7 NITs: 300/0.25 doc-comments (function-doc said "300 px base drop scaled by 0.25" but code is 200/0.4); leftover REMOVAL rationale block in CableOverlay constructor; stale `onVBlank` "now pumps a repaint every frame" comment (obsolete after the delta-gate fix); stale `CableMainOutPopup` reference at MixerPage.cpp:1037 (class was deleted but comment said "Reuse"); `mTopScrollBar` rename to `mHScrollBar` (scrollbar was relocated to bottom in this batch but member name + comments still said "top"); snapshot comment overstated `-inf`/NaN behavior (the floor is `-60.f` not `-inf`, so stable-equality not NaN is what suppresses repaint); `findStripByChannelId` perf note routed to `/perf-audit` (became FND-11 / Task 8 fold-in).  All fixed in-batch in Task 8 fix-up commit.
- **FND-16 (Master cable cutout / cable-over-Master bug — owner-verification finding)** — surfaced 2026-05-24 during owner-verification of the `/review-batch` + `/perf-audit` fix-up build.  Three interrelated visual bugs at Master strip: (a) cables that crossed Master's body bled over the strip ("EVERY AND I MEAN EVERY FUCKING STRIP should have it's send side disappear as it goes behind the master strip while scrolling and EVERY strip that goes into the master should still have THE RECEIVING SIDE ON THE MASTER SHOWING" — Jeff's spec); (b) cables to Master dimmed progressively as their source strips scrolled out of viewport (cables silently dropped from the visible bundle at Master's socket); (c) right-click chooser on the bundle at Master only listed cables whose sources were still on-screen (scroll-dependent listing).  Diagnosed across two iterations: first attempt was a path-derived clip-region carve-out (the carve-out IS the cable's bezier path) — failed when sources scrolled fully behind Master because then the cable's entire path was inside Master and the carve-out exposed it.  Second attempt switched to a fixed rectangle centered on Master's destination-socket position — scroll-safe because the rectangle pins to the dst regardless of where src scrolled to.  Dimensions tuned interactively via a transient "Tune Master Cutout" calibration UI mirroring the Rusty kit hitbox editor (toggle button + 8-handle drag-resize rectangle overlay + "Save Cutout Layout" → log file in `Documents/BaySickDAW/mixer_master_cutout.txt` in paste-able C++ form); final tuned values 5×14 px + 0.5/5.0 offset baked in as file-scope `static constexpr` (`kMasterCutoutW` / `kMasterCutoutH` / `kMasterCutoutXOff` / `kMasterCutoutYOff`) and the calibration UI fully removed at owner request.  Dimming fix: replaced `src.x < 0` skip in `renderMasterStub` + Pass B2 sends loop with exact-sentinel check (`src.x == -1.f && src.y == -1.f` only) so cables from off-screen-but-valid sources still render through the cutout at full color-depth.  Right-click chooser fix: added a Master-cutout pass at the end of `hitTestCablesAll` that lists every cable terminating at Master when the click point falls inside the cutout rectangle, de-duped against bezier-hit-test entries to avoid double-listing.  Net post-fix: cables disappear behind Master correctly + connection stubs visible at Master's socket + cable bundle stays full-density regardless of scroll + right-click chooser lists every routing-to-Master scroll-independent.  Owner-verified PASSED 2026-05-24 ("We're good").

### 2026-05-24 23:00 PT — QA-AudioMeters — Per-Insert Meter Unification (G1 standardization across all 8 InsertKinds)

**Bucket:** Cross-cutting Infrastructure, Mixer / Routing, System Pages

#### Done

QA-AudioMeters extended the QA-Eg G1 migration pattern to the per-insert metering surface — the last remaining instance of the centralized `PluginProcessor::*Run` mirror pattern after QA-Eg lifted the 8 G2 buses off it.  Originally scoped (per the §5 entry) as the per-row Builder audio meter migration only; mid-Task-1 pre-flight inventory surfaced that the source assumed by the spec didn't match reality (InsertNode publish was load-decay-max-store, not simple-store; a peakDbSnap snapshot-promotion layer sat between peakDb and the UI consumer; peakDbSnap had two consumers via the same drain function), driving an L7 re-spec from Sub-B Option B to **Option 2 — full restructure of the per-insert metering architecture across all 8 InsertKinds** (Layer / Bass / Drum / Audio / Aux / Vox / Inst / Rusty).  All 8 InsertKinds now use the same unified G1 publish chain the 12 G1 buses already use post-QA-Eg: `InsertNode publishPeakReading` (CAS-max + latency-comp ring) → `VibeGraph` public-member atomic (`processInsert` exchange-store + Task 5 fix-up CAS-max merge for multi-call-per-block correctness) → `PluginProcessor` mirror (`drainMeterAtomicsForUI` `drainAndMerge`) → UI poll (`VibeSynthProcessor::drainInsertPeakDbStereo` exchange-reset).  The pre-batch peakDbSnap snapshot-promotion layer was removed entirely from `InsertNode` (the per-insert half of `promoteAllInsertPeakSnapshots` is folded into the new G1 drain; the rack-promotion half survives under the renamed `promoteAllRackSlotSnapshots`); per L8 / Sub-C B2 the 6 force-reset stores in `renderAudioClipsForRow` / `renderFilePlayPlayer` were deleted so per-row Audio meters now decay over ~20ms via the `DBFSMeter` ballistic filter — matching every bus meter's mute behavior.  Net architectural change: the bus-vs-insert architectural inconsistency QA-Eg's bus migration left exposed is closed; all 20 publishing surfaces (12 buses + 8 InsertKinds) share one publish chain with no dual-path divergence.  Six commits across the four executable tasks plus close paperwork, all owner-verified Release+Debug clean in Jeff's per-task verify cycle.  L6 ran a two-phase task structure revision: original 5-task → revised to 10-task mid-Task-1 (per-kind verify per QA-Eg precedent) → re-collapsed to 6-task post-Task-2 when Jeff observed the per-kind verify workflow required impractical tab-switching; final shape **Task 0 open / Task 1 inventory / Task 2 structural / Task 3 stress-file verify / Task 4 cleanup / Task 5 close** (with fix-up + close commits inside Task 5 per QA-Eg's Task-8-multi-commit pattern).

- **Task 0 — open commit (docs only)** (`14400fb`): plan file `Plans & Specs/Batch Plans/mellow-bubbling-pancake.md` mirrored from `~/.claude/plans/` + home-dir copy deleted (per `feedback_plan_mirror_one_way.md`); §5 QA-AudioMeters `**Plan file:**` pointer flipped from the placeholder form to the backticked canonical path; running-notes seed `Plans & Specs/Running Notes/mellow-bubbling-pancake.md` created per §0 required-sections.  Five spec calls locked with Jeff 2026-05-24 at ExitPlanMode (**L1 = G1 migration pattern**, **L2 = per-row Builder audio meters scope** [later expanded via L7 re-spec], **L3 = surface inventory**, **L4 = sequencing after QA-Eg before QA-InsertMaps**, **L5 = silly-name `mellow-bubbling-pancake`**) + 3 sub-spec calls (**L6 = 5-task structure**, **L7 = Sub-B Option B**, **L8 = Sub-C B2** delete the 6 force-reset stores).

- **Task 1 — pre-flight inventory (read-only) + L7 pivot to Option 2** (`3c87264`, docs only): direct reads of all 7 inventory targets surfaced three architectural findings that invalidated L7 / Sub-B Option B's assumptions, driving an in-task re-spec to Option 2.  **F-1:** `InsertNode` publish at [`VibeGraph.cpp:1231-1242`](../Source/VibeGraph.cpp) was the open-coded InsertNode equivalent of the bus `publishPeakReading` helper (load-decay-max-store, not the plan's assumed simple-store) so the one-line CAS-max upgrade Option B prescribed for `:1241` wouldn't compose.  **F-2:** a `peakDbSnap` snapshot-promotion layer sat between `peakDb` and the UI consumer — `drainInsertPeakDbStereo` read `peakDbSnap` (NOT `peakDb` directly) and `promoteAllInsertPeakSnapshots` did the `peakDb → peakDbSnap` exchange, an extra mirror layer the per-bus G1 pattern does NOT have.  **F-3:** `peakDbSnap` had TWO consumers — `drainInsertPeakDbStereo` served BOTH the per-row Builder audio-meter surface AND the per-insert Mixer-strip surface (the `getInsertPeakDbStereoExchange` name in `publishPeakReading`'s comment at `VibeGraph.cpp:115` was a stale alias for the same function), so Option B's single exchange-store would corrupt one or both reads on consecutive polls.  **Jeff's re-spec at surface (verbatim, two phrases bracketing the direction):** "Let's embrace the scope" + "Let's do the surgery and get the architecture right".  **Spec calls revised:** L6 re-spec from 5-task to 10-task structure (Tasks 3-7 per-kind verify pattern mirroring QA-Eg's per-bus rhythm); L7 re-spec to Option 2 full restructure; **L9 NEW spec call** locking the PluginProcessor parallel mirrors as the UI poll target.  Effort + risk revised from low-medium / 3-5 hours to medium-high / 12-18 hours, scope-up acknowledged per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md`.

- **Task 2 — structural one-shot landed** (`0fd9b91`): the big source commit landing the L7-Option-2 architectural shift across **6 source files, +362 / -283 lines**.  Highlights: `InsertNode` struct rewritten (deleted `peakDbSnap` / `peakDbLSnap` / `peakDbRSnap` + `peakDecayDbPerBlock` + `kDecayDbPerSec`); `InsertNode::process`'s open-coded ~12-line load-decay-max-store publish replaced with a single `publishPeakReading(buf, peakRingL, peakRingR, peakRingIdx, peakDbL, peakDbR, peakDb)` call (matches bus pattern); per-kind exchange-store block added in `processInsert` after `node->processBlock` (8-way switch on `InsertKind` with bounds-checked `storeAxes` lambda); `promoteAllInsertPeakSnapshots` → `promoteAllRackSlotSnapshots` (insert-promotion half dropped, rack-promotion half kept); 3 legacy accessor bodies (~80 lines) deleted; 24 new VibeGraph public-member atomic arrays (8 kinds × 3 axes) + `kMaxAudioInserts = 50` constant + static_assert; 21 new PluginProcessor mirror arrays (Audio kind reuses existing `mAudioRowPeakDb*` per L9 / Builder backward compat); new ~55-line `VibeSynthProcessor::drainInsertPeakDbStereo` accessor; deleted the `arCasMax` lambda + per-flow drain block inside Flow B of `renderAudioClipsForRow`; **deleted all 6 force-reset stores per L8 / Sub-C B2**; rewrote `drainMeterAtomicsForUI`'s G3 per-row loop into 8 per-kind `drainAndMerge` loops; CompositeAudioInsertTask.cpp Flow A drain + CAS-max block (~17 lines) deleted; MixerPage.cpp `drainStereoInsert` lambda rewired + Audio-row inline exchange-reset unified with the other 7 kinds. #### Carry-forward contradictions

- **Carry-Forward Reference §1 (Render Engine Primitives) — per-insert metering G1/G2 split now fully gone.**  Mirrors the QA-Eg close note about the per-bus split: pre-QA-AudioMeters the per-insert surface published via the open-coded load-decay-max-store chain + peakDbSnap snapshot promotion + PluginProcessor `*Run` mirrors; post-QA-AudioMeters all 8 InsertKinds publish through the identical bus pattern.  No dual-path divergence anywhere on the metering surface.

- **peakDbSnap snapshot-promotion layer removed entirely from InsertNode.**  Pre-batch InsertNode had a 2-stage publish (write `peakDb` per audio block, promote to `peakDbSnap` once per UI frame via `promoteAllInsertPeakSnapshots`); post-batch the promotion half is gone, the rack-promotion half survives under the renamed `promoteAllRackSlotSnapshots`.

- **`peakDecayDbPerBlock` field gone from InsertNode** (parallel to QA-Eg's bus-side deletion).  Decay is now handled by `DBFSMeter` ballistic in the UI layer, not at publish.

- **6 force-reset stores deleted from `renderAudioClipsForRow` + `renderFilePlayPlayer` (L8 / Sub-C B2).**  Per-row Audio meters no longer snap to `-60.f` on mute / choke / file-end early-returns; instead they decay over ~20ms via the `DBFSMeter` ballistic — aligns per-row Audio meter mute response to every bus meter's mute response.

### 2026-05-25 23:30 PT — QA-InsertMaps — Flatten InsertNode std::map to std::array (perf-audit H1)

**Bucket:** Cross-cutting Infrastructure, Mixer / Routing

#### Done

QA-InsertMaps is the second batch in the QA-Eg-close-spawned perf-audit cluster (after QA-AudioMeters; before QA-VoicePool + QA-EngineApvts).  It flattens the 8 `std::map<int, std::unique_ptr<InsertNode>>` member tables on `VibeGraph` (one per `InsertKind`: `Layer / Bass / Drum / Audio / Aux / Vox / Inst / Rusty`) into a single owning `std::array<std::unique_ptr<InsertNode>, kMaxStripChannels = 1000> mInsertsByChannel` indexed directly by ChannelId, paired with a `std::vector<int> mLiveInsertChannels` companion list for the 14 iteration sites that previously walked individual maps.  The `selectInsertMap` anon-namespace switch helper at the pre-batch [VibeGraph.cpp:2253-2277](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:2253) is deleted; the per-kind `(kind, index) -> ChannelId` switch inside the audio-thread `processInsert` at the pre-batch :2343-2353 also dies because `InsertNode` now caches its own `chId` at construction (Sub-H / L9).  External `(kind, index)` accessor API at the 30+ consumer sites across 6 files preserved verbatim per L7 / Sub-B (zero call-site churn) — the wrappers route through a new `static inline int computeChannelId(InsertKind, int)` helper hoisted to the top of `VibeGraph.cpp`.  Net architectural change: ends the bus-vs-insert architectural inconsistency between `RenderGraphDispatcher::mTasksByChannel` (already flat-array-by-ChannelId in-tree pre-batch) and the InsertNode side (8 std::maps); hot-path lookups become single-pointer indirection.  Estimated CPU win ~1-3% on busy sessions per `/perf-audit` H1 (NOT measured this batch — empirical measurement would be a separate task; the architectural alignment justifies the batch even if the win isn't observable).  Also absorbs perf-audit M3 (UI-side `getInsertPeakDb` per-vblank `std::map::find`) — same flattening eliminates both lookups.  Four source commits across the executable tasks plus close paperwork; all owner-verified Release+Debug clean in Jeff's per-task verify cycle.  Final task structure per L11 / Sub-F locked at Task 0 = the proven 6-task QA-AudioMeters mirror: **Task 0 open / Task 1 inventory / Task 2 structural / Task 3 stress-file verify / Task 4 cleanup / Task 5 close** (Task 3 verify-only per L10 / Sub-E, no source commit).

- **Task 0 — open commit (docs only)** (`fbdc0e0`): plan file [Plans & Specs/Batch Plans/zany-wandering-russell.md](Plans & Specs/Batch Plans/zany-wandering-russell.md) mirrored from `~/.claude/plans/` + home-dir copy deleted (per `feedback_plan_mirror_one_way.md`); §5 QA-InsertMaps `**Plan file:**` pointer flipped from the placeholder form to the backticked canonical path; running-notes seed [Plans & Specs/Running Notes/zany-wandering-russell.md](Plans & Specs/Running Notes/zany-wandering-russell.md) created per §0 required-sections.  Five spec calls locked with Jeff at ExitPlanMode (**L1 = Option 2** single flat array by ChannelId mirroring `RenderGraphDispatcher::mTasksByChannel`, **L2 = kMaxStripChannels = 1000**, **L3 = sequencing after QA-AudioMeters before QA-VoicePool**, **L4 = silly-name `zany-wandering-russell`**, **L5 = MT verification cadence normal Debug-then-Release per-task**) + 6 sub-spec calls (Sub-A / L6 / Sub-B / L7 / Sub-C / L8 / Sub-D / L9 / Sub-E / L10 / Sub-F / L11) re-surfaced via plain-English numbered list per `feedback_design_approval_in_plain_english.md` extension after Jeff dismissed both the recommendations-in-plan-file approach and the `AskUserQuestion` box ("Please write them all out with their options instead of giving me the box its so much easier to review everything at once"); all 6 picked Option (a) ("Your analysis is flawless... Option A across the board").  Process lesson captured for `feedback_dont_make_unilateral_spec_calls.md` reinforcement: recommendations in a plan file do NOT substitute for explicit per-call resolution at ExitPlanMode, and for multi-question batches (>2 questions) plain-text discrete-question presentation beats the `AskUserQuestion` box for batch review.

- **Task 1 — pre-flight inventory (read-only)** (`cb40412`, docs only): complete `VibeGraph.cpp` call-site map appended to running notes as a 24-row inventory table sorted by line number. #### Found along the way

- **FND-1 (Task 1 inventory — §5 entry's "4x map.find per insert per block" claim confirmed; full surface mapped to 24-row inventory table)** — 8 `.find` sites + 14 iteration sites + 1 `.size` + 1 `.clear`.  **NEW findings beyond §5 + Task 0 grep:** `getInsertChokeGroup` 2nd `selectInsertMap`-style switch at the pre-batch [VibeGraph.cpp:2507-2526](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:2507), `isAnyInsertSoloed` at the pre-batch :2566-2572, `rebuildRoutingFromApvts` at the pre-batch :2899-2962, `getAuxIndices()` at the pre-batch :2966-2972, `clearAuxInserts()` at the pre-batch :2975-2978.

- **FND-2 (Task 1 Finding A — `prepare()` loop at the pre-batch [VibeGraph.cpp:1364-1366](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:1364) excludes Rusty)** — pointer-init-list `{ &mLayerInserts, &mBassInserts, &mDrumInserts, &mAudioInserts, &mAuxInserts, &mVoxInserts, &mInstInserts }` — 7 of 8 kinds.  Likely defensive (Rusty inserts get prepared on construction via `ensureInsertNode` if `mSampleRate > 0.0`; the `prepare()` sweep is a fallback for inserts that existed before `prepareToPlay`).  Non-risk; Task 2 silently fixed via `mLiveInsertChannels` iteration (covers all 8 kinds naturally; the duplicate `prepare()` is safe).

- **FND-3 (Task 1 Finding B — `reset()` loop at the pre-batch [VibeGraph.cpp:1409-1411](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:1409) excludes Vox/Inst/Rusty)** — pointer-init-list `{ &mLayerInserts, &mBassInserts, &mDrumInserts, &mAudioInserts, &mAuxInserts }` — 5 of 8 kinds.  Pre-existing oversight when R1 (Vox/Inst, 2026-04-23) and J-4 (Rusty, 2026-05-03) extended the `InsertKind` enum without updating the 5F-4a reset loop.  Surfaced to Jeff at Task 2 plan-finalize as **Sub-G** spec call (3 options: preserve 5-kind filter / include all 8 / preserve + §9 Forks investigation); Jeff picked Option 2 (include all 8) WITH a pre-execution `InsertNode::reset()` sanity check.

- **FND-4 (Task 1 — InsertNode ctor chId-source spec call)** — Sub-D / L9 said "InsertNode caches its own chId at construction" but left open whether the ctor takes `chId` as a parameter OR computes it internally via `MixerChannelIds::<kind>Insert(idx)` (which would require `InsertNode` to include `MixerChannelIds.h`).  Surfaced to Jeff at Task 2 plan-finalize as **Sub-H** spec call; Jeff picked Option (a) ctor-parameter form (`ensureInsertNode` computes chId once for the lookup, passes to ctor; keeps InsertNode dependency-light).

- **FND-5 (Task 2 plan-finalize Sub-G sanity check — Finding C: `VibeGraph::reset()` is dead code)** — grep for `mVibeGraph.reset / vibeGraph.reset / vg.reset / &VibeGraph::reset` across `Source/` returned ZERO matches.  PluginProcessor's `.reset()` calls are all on `std::unique_ptr<Task>` instances (releasing tasks), never on `mVibeGraph`.  The 5-kind asymmetry from Finding B never caused a runtime bug because the function never runs.  Jeff routed: Sub-G Option 2 implemented anyway (clean end state) + new **§9 thirty-sixth Forks entry** appended to Main Plan to investigate wiring `VibeGraph::reset()` to transport Stop in a future dedicated batch (verbatim Title / Context / Impact / Action from Jeff wrapped in §0 conventional scaffolding); QA-InsertMaps does NOT delete the `reset()` function (scope-creep avoidance).

- **FND-9 (Task 5 open — per-task `/draft-doc running-notes` appends were skipped for Tasks 2/3/4 during live execution)** — deviation from `feedback_draft_doc_running_notes_every_checkpoint.md`.  Caught at Task 5 open when reviewing the running-notes file for `/draft-doc batch-close` input; consolidated catch-up appended pre-close (one consolidated entry covering all 3 tasks).  Process lesson captured in the running-notes catch-up entry: every commit landing closes the task's `[ ]` checkbox in the plan AND fires the running-notes append BEFORE moving to the next task; if a task has no source commit (e.g. Task 3 verify-only), the append still fires at PASS-confirmation.  No process change needed at the plan-file level (the plan's "Dispatch `/draft-doc running-notes` and apply" step is already in every task's checkbox list) — the gap was execution discipline, not plan-text.

- **FND-10 (`/review-batch` Task 5 close-pass — `rebuildRoutingFromApvts` reserve hint off-by-one + comment miscount)** — at [VibeGraph.cpp:2938](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:2938), my new `mActiveChannels.reserve(13 + mLiveInsertChannels.size())` + adjacent comment says "reserve 13 buses (master + 12 buses)".  Counting the `emplace_back` block at :2940-2958 there are exactly **12** emplaces total (Master IS one of the 12 buses, not separate).  Functionally harmless (over-reserving is fine), but mirrors the same "12 vs 13" miscount QA-Eg / QA-AudioMeters carried in their commit messages.  Introduced by my Task 2 edit at `eb718bf`.  Initially marked for close-entry deferral; reclassified mid-Task-5 per Jeff's anti-defer directive + fixed in fix-up `e9fe545` (reserve hint corrected to `12 +` + comment rewrites the "13 buses" miscount + ladders the 12 bus emplaces in the same comment for future readers).

- **FND-11 (Task 5 fix-up full-warning rebuild — 2 pre-existing C4505 dead-helper warnings surfaced)** — surfaced when the fix-up's NIT 1 fix removed the C4189 noise covering them: `calcBusGain` (3-arg helper computing fader × mute × solo bus gain) was orphaned by QA-Ea Part A's bus-solo cached-atomic restructure (the gain computation moved inline into the bus-process hot paths so the helper has zero callers); `bufferPeakDb` mono variant (single-channel peak-dB reader) was orphaned by QA-Eg/QA-AudioMeters' G1 stereo publish chain (the stereo `bufferPeakDbStereo` variant subsumed the mono helper at every call site).  Both pre-existing pre-QA-InsertMaps; surfaced BY this batch's fix-up rebuild, not introduced.  Per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md` "fix-don't-park" disposition: folded both deletions into the same fix-up commit `e9fe545` rather than a separate sweep.

- **FND-12 (Task 5 fix-up — MSBuild stale-`.obj` cache after dead-helper deletions)** — after deleting `calcBusGain` + `bufferPeakDb` (mono) from source, C4505 warnings persisted in the subsequent build.  Source verified clean via grep + `git diff` + `awk` (no surviving definitions or callers); `.obj` modification times were post-source, suggesting MSBuild's incremental build cache returned the stale compiler output.  Fixed by force-deleting `build/BaySickDAWStandalone.dir/{Release,Debug}/VibeGraph.obj` and forcing fresh recompile; subsequent build clean (only `VibeGraph.cpp` recompiled, no C4505 noise).  Build-system gotcha worth remembering for future "deleted but warning still appears" debug cycles: trust the source, suspect the cache.

My initial routing of NIT 3 as "explain 3 for decision" deferred to Jeff's call after I walked through the forward-compat hazard: pre-batch the unknown label dropped silently (per-kind map's `find` returned `end`, skip); my Task 2 Layer-fallback **introduced** a subtle regression that could corrupt Layer state for future-added kinds whose XML labels land in saved projects.  The optional-return pattern restores the pre-batch skip semantics + adds explicit nullopt-handling at the call site.  Header `#include <optional>` added to `VibeGraph.cpp`. |
| NIT 4: `removeInsertNode` O(n) `std::find` on `mLiveInsertChannels` at [VibeGraph.cpp:2371-2373](Source/VibeGraph.cpp:2371) | **Acknowledged as L8/Sub-C accepted design.**  Not a deferral — design-locked at Task 0 ExitPlanMode (Jeff's pick: single companion list with per-iter `node->kind` dispatch).  Rare-event message-thread surface (~20-50 live inserts max; remove fires on strip-delete only, not in audio-thread hot path).  Future cleanup could swap to `std::unordered_set<int>` companion if profiling ever surfaces a hot site — not warranted by current data; reframed in fix-up commit message as accepted design, not "deferred". |

**Fix-up build cycle gotcha:** after deleting `calcBusGain` + `bufferPeakDb` (mono) from source, C4505 warnings persisted in the subsequent build despite source verified clean via grep + `git diff` + `awk`.  `.obj` modification times were post-source, suggesting MSBuild's incremental build cache returned stale compiler output.  Fixed by force-deleting `build/BaySickDAWStandalone.dir/{Release,Debug}/VibeGraph.obj` and forcing fresh recompile; subsequent build clean (only `VibeGraph.cpp` recompiled, no C4505 noise).  Build-system gotcha worth remembering for future "deleted but warning still appears" debug cycles: trust the source, suspect the cache.

### 2026-05-26 17:30 PT — QA-VoicePool — Pre-allocated VibePlayer Voice Pool / Object Pool (perf-audit H3)

**Bucket:** Players, Cross-cutting Infrastructure

#### Done

QA-VoicePool is the third batch in the QA-Eg-close-spawned perf-audit cluster (after QA-AudioMeters at `65f57ad` and QA-InsertMaps at `3587ade`; before the close-spawned QA-SfzGroup detour and then QA-EngineApvts). It eliminates audio-thread heap allocation on every VibePlayer note-on by moving `VibeVoice::startNote` off the pre-batch per-note `std::make_unique<juce::MemoryAudioSource>` + `std::make_unique<ReversedMemoryAudioSource>` + `std::make_unique<juce::ResamplingAudioSource>` pattern (3 allocations per note-on at the pre-batch [VibePlayerDSP.cpp:581 / :583 / :607](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:581)) onto a fat-voice pool where each `VibeVoice` owns forward + reverse source readers + dual permanent `juce::ResamplingAudioSource` instances as direct members and `startNote` re-points the active-source / active-resampler pointer pair via `setBuffer` + `flushBuffers` + `setResamplingRatio`. Also collapses the fourth audio-thread heap-alloc site at [VibePlayerDSP.cpp:415](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:415) — `VibeSampleManager::findRegion`'s `std::vector<int> candidates; candidates.reserve(8)` swapped to a `std::array<int, 32>` stack-alloc with bounded-write overflow handling per L5(a). With Task 2 + Task 3 + Task 4 in, all 4 audio-thread per-note heap-alloc sites identified in the original §9 thirty-fourth Forks blueprint are closed — the per-note allocation surface for VibePlayer is fully zero. Net architectural change: VibePlayer's voice pool (already 16 voices via `juce::Synthesiser` framework per L1=(a) "refactor INSIDE juce::Synthesiser framework") now has FAT voices, the voiceCap-stealing path moves off hot-path `dynamic_cast<VibeVoice*>(mSynth.getVoice(i))` scans (which fired on every unison fan-out iteration of every note-on) onto a cached `std::array<VibeVoice*, kMaxVoices> mVoices` direct pointer cache populated in the VibeSynth ctor alongside each `mSynth.addVoice(v)`, and the steal-victim selection logic upgrades from oldest-first to a 3-tier hybrid (release > noteOff-queued-or-key-released > key-down-PROTECTED with oldest-within-tier). Estimated runtime win is the elimination of 3-4 heap allocations on every single note-on across every VibePlayer engine instance in a session — the absolute hottest path in the app (drum hits + key presses + piano-roll triggers + auditions) — NOT measured empirically this batch; the architectural goal is closing the per-note allocation surface, not chasing a profile-confirmed number. Seven source / docs / tooling commits across the executable tasks plus close paperwork; all owner-verified Release+Debug clean in Jeff's per-task verify cycle. Final task structure per L8(b) split: Task 0 open / Task 1 inventory + commit-mechanics rule fold / Task 2 fat-voice structural / Task 3 lock-free occupancy + stealing + ADSR quick-release / Task 4 findRegion stack-alloc + SFZ `<group>` finding surface / Task 5 stress-file verify (no commit per L10/Sub-E) / Task 6 cleanup + grep sweep / Task 7 NIT fix-up + close.
