- **Task 3 — lock-free occupancy + L7(b) 3-tier hybrid stealing + L6 ADSR quick-release via physical over-provisioning + look-ahead noteOff pre-scan** (`0dcfe50`, 2 files, +247/-23): Task 3's deliverable shape evolved through THREE Jeff-driven course corrections during execution. The final ship is a strict superset of the locked L6 + L7(b) wording — same goals, refined mechanism. **Correction 1 (synchronous-render fade-out → physical over-provisioning + ADSR quick-release):** initial Task 3 implementation prepared `VibeVoice::initiateSteal()` as a 64-sample synchronous render INTO the output buffer at MIDI-dispatch time — the literal reading of L6=(d). Jeff caught the fundamental safety flaw at design-surface time pre-commit, verbatim: "Rendering 64 samples forward synchronously during an event callback is fundamentally unsafe. If the audio block size is small (e.g., 32 samples) and a steal happens near the end of the block, writing 64 samples will cause a buffer overflow and crash the DAW." Pivoted to **physical over-provisioning**: `kMaxVoices` raised 16 → 24 (the juce::Synthesiser pool now holds 24 fat voices, NOT 16); new `static constexpr int kLogicalCap = 16` added (user-facing polyphony default unchanged; existing `voiceCap` APVTS range 1..16 unchanged); 8 reserve voices (`kMaxVoices - kLogicalCap`) are invisible to the user — they exist solely as overflow slots for stolen-voice fade-outs. `VibeVoice::initiateSteal()` overrides the voice's `mAdsr.release` to 0.0015 sec (~66 samples @ 44.1 kHz, ~72 samples @ 48 kHz — matches the L6=(d) 64-sample target closely); saves user's pre-steal ADSR params into `mPreStealAdsrParams` + sets `mAdsrOverridden = true` for next-startNote restore. Steal calls `victim->initiateSteal(); victim->stopNote(0.f, true)` — `allowTailOff=true` lets the voice enter its (now quick) ADSR release naturally; fade-out runs sample-accurately inside the subsequent `juce::Synthesiser::renderNextBlock`'s standard per-voice render loop. Zero buffer-overflow risk: JUCE's render loop bounds writes to the actual buffer length, fade-out continues across blocks as needed, `clearCurrentNote()` fires at ADSR end exactly as for any natural noteOff release. New note allocates to one of the 8 reserve voices via `juce::Synthesiser::findFreeVoice` (the just-stolen voice is still `isVoiceActive() == true` during its fade-out, so JUCE picks a different free voice). 8 extra VibeVoice instances cost ~120 KB per VibePlayer engine instance for the dual-permanent-resampler-state RAM alone — acceptable for the safety property gained. **Correction 2 (2-tier hybrid → 3-tier with `isKeyDown()` protection):** initial L7(b) implementation was the literal 2-tier wording — "prefer release-phase oldest, fallback to overall-oldest". Jeff identified the held-lead + looping-chord pathology at verify time, verbatim: "If I hold down a sustained lead note (a 5th note), and let a 4-note chord sequence loop repeatedly in the background, my sustained lead note is eventually stolen. Because it was held down across multiple bars, it becomes the 'oldest active note,' so the engine kills it to make room for the newer chords. The Pro DAW Standard: voice stealing is based on a hierarchy that respects physical key state (Note-On vs. Note-Off). An actively held note should almost never be stolen by newer notes that have already received their Note-Off commands." Upgraded to **3-tier hybrid** using JUCE's built-in `juce::SynthesiserVoice::isKeyDown()` accessor (verified public at juce_Synthesiser.h:233 — no need for VibeVoice to track its own physical-key-state flag). Tier 0 = `isInRelease()` (already in ADSR release; voice is on its way out); Tier 1 = `isNoteOffQueued() || !isKeyDown()` (received noteOff or noteOff queued this block but not yet in release — sustain pedal hold etc.); Tier 2 = `isKeyDown() == true && !isNoteOffQueued()` (physically held, no queued noteOff; PROTECTED — only stolen as last resort). Within each tier, oldest by `mNoteStartCounter`. **Correction 3 (MIDI-event-ordering at loop boundaries → look-ahead noteOff pre-scan):** Jeff re-verified post-Correction-2 and reported the lead still stolen "after 2 bars" on the looping-chord scenario. Diagnosis: at a piano-roll loop boundary, the old chord's noteOffs and the new chord's noteOns land in the same MIDI buffer. `VibeSynth::renderNextBlock` dispatches noteOns IMMEDIATELY via `mSynth.noteOn` (which fires the voiceCap-stealing branch) but DEFERS noteOffs into `filteredMidi` for the bottom-of-function `mSynth.renderNextBlock(buffer, filteredMidi)` dispatch. At the moment of the new-chord-note voiceCap decision, the old chord voices are STILL `isKeyDown() == true` (their noteOffs haven't been dispatched yet — sitting in `filteredMidi` waiting); all voices appear as Tier 2 to `findStealCandidate`; Tier 2 protection layer protects no one; findStealCandidate picks the genuinely oldest = the held lead. Two alternatives surfaced (pre-pass-rewrite vs look-ahead-flag); Jeff's verbatim pick: "We absolutely must go with the Look-Ahead Pre-Scan. Losing sample-accurate MIDI timing (the pre-pass alternative) introduces MIDI jitter, which is unacceptable for a pro DAW. Preserving sub-block timing is entirely worth the extra ~25 lines of code." Fix: ~25-line look-ahead pre-scan at the top of `VibeSynth::renderNextBlock` walks the MIDI buffer ONCE BEFORE the noteOn dispatch loop, marking voices whose noteOffs are pending this block with a transient `mNoteOffQueued = true` flag (also correctly handles the same-pitch-strip case: if a later same-pitch noteOn would strip the noteOff via existing preemption, the flag is NOT set — no actual noteOff coming). `findStealCandidate`'s Tier 1 picks up the flagged voices alongside `!isKeyDown()`. Does NOT reorder events; actual MIDI dispatch order unchanged; sample-accurate timing preserved. Source edits landed: [Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.h](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.h) new VibeVoice public accessors (`isActive()` atomic load, `isInRelease()`, `isNoteOffQueued()` + setter, `initiateSteal()`); new VibeVoice private members (`std::atomic<bool> mIsActive { false }` per Sub-A=(a), `bool mInRelease { false }` per L7(b) Tier 0, `juce::ADSR::Parameters mPreStealAdsrParams {}` + `bool mAdsrOverridden { false }` per Correction 1, `bool mNoteOffQueued { false }` per Correction 3); VibeSynth changes (`kMaxVoices = 24` was 16, NEW `kLogicalCap = 16`, NEW `std::array<VibeVoice*, kMaxVoices> mVoices` direct pointer cache, NEW `findStealCandidate(int newPitch)` private 3-tier scan, `forEachVoice` template refactored to iterate `mVoices[]` instead of dynamic_casting). [Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp) VibeSynth ctor populates `mVoices[i] = v` alongside each `mSynth.addVoice(v)`; `VibeVoice::setAdsr` routes new user setting into `mPreStealAdsrParams` when `mAdsrOverridden == true` (preserves in-flight 1.5 ms quick-release; applies new user setting on next startNote via restore path); `VibeVoice::startNote` restores user's ADSR params if `mAdsrOverridden == true` then clears, plus `mIsActive.store(true, release)` + `mInRelease = false`; `VibeVoice::stopNote(velocity, allowTailOff=true)` sets `mInRelease = true` BEFORE `mAdsr.noteOff()`; `VibeVoice::releaseResources` clears `mIsActive` + `mInRelease` (`mAdsrOverridden` + `mPreStealAdsrParams` persist — startNote is the natural restore point per NIT 4 rationale); NEW `VibeVoice::initiateSteal()` (idempotent override); NEW `VibeSynth::findStealCandidate()` (3-tier scan); NEW ~25-line look-ahead noteOff pre-scan at top of `VibeSynth::renderNextBlock`; same-pitch preemption loop at pre-batch :899-906 rewritten to read `mVoices[vi]` instead of `dynamic_cast<VibeVoice*>(mSynth.getVoice(vi))`; voiceCap-stealing branch at pre-batch :944-960 full rewrite (active-voice count scan reading `mIsActive` atomics + `findStealCandidate(note)` call + `victim->initiateSteal(); victim->stopNote(0.f, true)`); critical fix-as-you-go: `cap` calculation changed from `mLastVoiceCap > 0 ? mLastVoiceCap : kMaxVoices` to `... : kLogicalCap` (was kMaxVoices) — with kMaxVoices=24 post-Correction-1, leaving cap=kMaxVoices by default would have given cap=24, defeating the entire over-provisioning intent. Grep post-edits: `grep -rn "dynamic_cast<VibeVoice" Source/VibePlayer/` returns ZERO matches — all hot-path voice scans use `mVoices[]` direct pointer cache per Sub-A=(a). Both Release + Debug build clean. Jeff-verified PASS across 6 scenarios (A: 16-chord-loop + held lead default voiceCap=16, B: 4-chord-loop + held lead voiceCap=4, C: release-phase-preferred regression voiceCap=4, D: all-keys-held fallback regression voiceCap=4, E: Reverse mode + Scenarios A/B, F: MT-on vs MT-off parity).

- **Task 4 — findRegion `std::array<int, 32>` stack-alloc + pre-existing SFZ `<group>` parser bug surfaced** (`add0bfc`, 1 file, +22/-11): L5(a) source edit, fully contained inside `VibeSampleManager::findRegion`. Single surgical block + three loop-body conversions: allocation site at the pre-batch [:415-416](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:415) replaced `std::vector<int> candidates; candidates.reserve(8)` with `constexpr int kMaxCandidates = 32; std::array<int, kMaxCandidates> candidates {}; int numCandidates = 0;`; push site uses bounded `if (numCandidates < kMaxCandidates) candidates[numCandidates++] = i` (implements Jeff's L5(a) overflow lock — 33rd+ candidate silently dropped, no jassert / no log, "UX-acceptable degradation for an edge case that should never fire on Jeff's libraries"); emptiness check `candidates.empty()` → `numCandidates == 0`; iteration sites (hasRR pre-scan + round-robin selection) both range-fors replaced with index-counted `for (int j = 0; j < numCandidates; ++j) { int idx = candidates[j]; ... }`. The 4th and final heap-alloc site identified at Task 1 inventory is now closed; the audio-thread per-note allocation surface for VibePlayer is fully zero per the original §9 thirty-fourth Forks blueprint scope. #### Found along the way

- **FND-1 (Task 2 verify — BaySickSynth `mOsc.reset()` / `mOsc2.reset()` missing in `BaySickSynthVoice::startNote`)** — diagnosed during a misdirected first-verify attempt on BaySickSynth (out of Task 2 scope; QA-VoicePool is BaySickPlayer-only per L1=(a) + §5 entry's scope). Jeff observed waveform varying between consecutive chord hits in a Fresh Test arrangement recording at `Projects/Fresh Test/Samples/Fresh Test - Master - 2026-05-25 15-03-07.wav`. I initially handwaved as "pre-existing behavior" — Jeff correctly overruled. Real bug, not Task 2's. Root cause: `BaySickSynthVoice` owns two `WavetableOscillator` members `mOsc` + `mOsc2` at [BaySickSynthVoice.h:122-123](Source/BaySickSynth/BaySickSynthVoice.h:122); `WavetableOscillator` has internal `float mPhase { 0.0f }` at WavetableOscillator.h:38 + exposed `void reset()` at WavetableOscillator.h:20; `BaySickSynthVoice::startNote` at [BaySickSynthVoice.cpp:36-123](Source/BaySickSynth/BaySickSynthVoice.cpp:36) resets inline phase accumulators (`mPhase1` / `mPhase2` / `mPhase3` / `mFMCarrierPhase` / `mFMModPhase` / `mDeafSawState` at :72-77) but does NOT call `mOsc.reset()` or `mOsc2.reset()` — wavetable phase persists across notes, same MIDI note replayed on same voice slot produces different starting wavetable phase → different waveform shape → audibly different sound per hit. Affected waveforms (use `mOsc` / `mOsc2`): SAW (default), SAW+SAW, SAW+SQUARE, SQUARE+SQUARE, SUPERSAW. Inline-phase waveforms (PULSE, BELL, DEAF SAW, SPREAD OCT, SPREAD 5TH, SINE) reset deterministically — unaffected. Process lesson: `feedback_check_code_before_calling_it_expected.md` applies — should have asked which engine BEFORE handwaving. Spec-call surface: (1) new dedicated batch / (2) fold into QA-EngineApvts / (3) fold into QA-VoicePool close-routing / (4) §9 Forks only, slot later. **Jeff's decision: Option (2) — fold into QA-EngineApvts.** Verbatim: "We need to strictly enforce our rollback boundaries. QA-VoicePool is about lock-free memory allocation for the sample player. I do not want to introduce synth DSP state changes into this commit." Scope-discipline lock — QA-VoicePool stays scope-pure.

- **FND-2 (Task 4 verify — pre-existing SFZ `<group>`-scoped opcode inheritance broken in `VibeSampleManager::parseSFZ`)** — surfaced when Jeff smoke-tested Tuba-KS.sfz post-Task-4 and reported round-robin variation was NOT clearly audible: "they all sound the same though and I've never noticed it making any sort of variation like that." Jeff explicitly asked for a two-track investigation: "To confirm I don't just mean missing in the SFZ file but in how we are reading them so check both." **Track 1 — file inspection:** Tuba-KS.sfz DOES specify 4-variant round-robin via `<group>`-scoped `seq_length=4` + `seq_position=1..4` opcodes per SFZ v1 spec; file content is correct. **Track 2 — parser inspection:** the bug is in our parser. The line-walking state machine in [`parseSFZ` at :90](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:90) sets `inRegion = true` only on `<region>` headers (at [:120](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:120)); on `<group>` headers it resets `inRegion = false` (at [:106](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:106)). The per-line opcode-extraction block contains an early-return `if (!inRegion) continue;` at [:148](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:148) BEFORE any opcode-write to the current region. Result: **every opcode inside a `<group>` block (including `seq_length` / `seq_position` and any other group-scoped inherited setting) is silently dropped**. Per the SFZ v1 spec, opcodes inside `<group>` are supposed to be inherited by every `<region>` that follows until the next `<group>` or EOF — our parser implements ZERO of this inheritance. For Tuba-KS specifically: every region's `seq_position` is dropped → defaults to `seq_position=0` (no rotation gate) → `findRegion` sees one candidate per `(note, velocity, artic)` tuple → no rotation possible → the 4-variant cycling specified in the file never reaches the audio thread. Audible result: "they all sound the same." **Pre-existing bug, NOT a Task 4 regression.** The early-return predates QA-VoicePool entirely; Task 4 only touched `findRegion`, not `parseSFZ`. Also completely independent of QA-VoicePool's scope — parser runs on the message thread (file load time), not the audio thread (heap-alloc territory); the reason Task 4's verify caught it is that the verify exercised the candidate list directly: the new stack-alloc populates the same data the old vector did, but with the data corrupted upstream by missing inheritance, the populated list is degenerate. Spec-call surface: (1) new dedicated batch / (2) fold into QA-VoicePool close-routing / (3) fold into downstream batch / (4) §9 Forks only, slot later. **Jeff's decision: Option (1) — new dedicated batch (QA-SfzGroup), slotted as the very next batch after QA-VoicePool closes.** Verbatim: "Incredible catch... We are going with Option 1: New dedicated batch (e.g., QA-SfzGroup). I want this fixed, but our strict rollback boundaries must remain intact. This current batch is strictly about real-time audio-thread heap allocations. The SFZ loader is a text parser running on the message thread. Let's queue up QA-SfzGroup to be our very next batch after we close QA-VoicePool. That batch will cover fixing the `<group>` state-machine inheritance and investigating the Aria/sfizz RR loss." Mirror of FND-1's scope-discipline rationale.

- **FND-3 (Task 3 design — L6 literal "64-sample synchronous fade-out" unsafe at small block sizes; pivoted to physical over-provisioning + ADSR quick-release)** — see Done > Task 3 > Correction 1 narrative. Not a NIT or routed finding; the pivot landed in Task 3's structural commit as the as-shipped mechanism. Recorded here for the post-batch reader so the deliverable-vs-literal-blueprint delta is visible.

- **FND-4 (Task 3 verify — 2-tier L7(b) hybrid stealing kills held leads at looping-chord-loop pathology)** — see Done > Task 3 > Correction 2 narrative. Not a NIT or routed finding; the 3-tier upgrade landed in Task 3's structural commit. Strict superset of L7(b) — same release-preferred bias, adds Tier 2 protect-held-keys layer.

- **FND-5 (Task 3 re-verify — MIDI event ordering at loop boundaries defeats 3-tier protection)** — see Done > Task 3 > Correction 3 narrative. Not a NIT or routed finding; the ~25-line look-ahead noteOff pre-scan at top of `VibeSynth::renderNextBlock` landed in Task 3's structural commit. Preserves sample-accurate MIDI timing per Jeff's verbatim "Preserving sub-block timing is entirely worth the extra ~25 lines of code."

- **FND-6 (Task 1 inventory — `juce::ResamplingAudioSource::setSource()` does NOT exist; Sub-B framing pivoted)** — load-bearing JUCE API finding from the read-only inventory pass that invalidated the plan's original Sub-B(a) framing. Direct read of `juce/modules/juce_audio_basics/sources/juce_ResamplingAudioSource.h` showed the input source pointer is fixed at construction (private `OptionalScopedPointer<AudioSource> input` member at :90). Revised Sub-B options surface (dual permanent resamplers vs custom VibeSourceFork adapter vs Jeff-proposed-different) landed in the Task 1 plan-file Edit + got resolved as Sub-B=(a) at Task 2 plan-finalize per Jeff's RAM-vs-virtual-dispatch tradeoff lock.

Not a sub-spec call; just a Task 3 implementation hook surfaced at inventory time so Task 3 didn't get stuck without a workaround.

- **FND-10 (Task 3 fix-as-you-go — `cap` calculation default fallback would consume reserve voices)** — surfaced during Task 3 implementation. Pre-correction the voiceCap-stealing branch computed `const int cap = mLastVoiceCap > 0 ? mLastVoiceCap : kMaxVoices`. With `kMaxVoices = 24` post-Correction-1, leaving the fallback at `kMaxVoices` would default `cap` to 24, defeating the entire over-provisioning intent (the 8 reserve voices would be available for user-facing polyphony, not for stealing-fade-out overflow). Fixed in-task: fallback changed to `kLogicalCap = 16`. The user-set APVTS `voiceCap` range (1..16) was already correctly clamped; this fix is for the default-no-APVTS-set path only. Caught before the Task 3 commit; no post-commit fix-up needed for this site. NIT 1 (see Done > Task 7) closed a related sibling hazard at `setVoiceCap`'s clamp.

|---|---|
| NIT 1: `VibeSynth::setVoiceCap` clamp upper bound at [VibePlayerDSP.cpp:1360](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:1360) — wrote `juce::jlimit(1, kMaxVoices, cap)` where `kMaxVoices = 24` post-Task-3-Correction-1, but the user-facing voiceCap APVTS range is 1..16 (kLogicalCap). Clamp doesn't fire today because the APVTS range never exceeds kLogicalCap, but a future range bump OR a saved-state `voiceCap=20` from a future version would consume reserve voices and break the fade-out mechanic. | **Fixed in fix-up `36fe7fb`.** Clamp upper bound changed `kMaxVoices` → `kLogicalCap`. 7-line explanatory comment added documenting the kMaxVoices vs kLogicalCap distinction + why kLogicalCap is the right value here even though kMaxVoices is the physical pool size. Jeff verbatim: "We absolutely must protect those 8 reserve voices to ensure the fade-out mechanic never breaks." |
| NIT 2: JUCE's `findVoiceToSteal` fallback may run in 25-note 24-voice catastrophic overflow + pick a different victim than L7(b)'s findStealCandidate. | **Reframed as accepted design.** Verbatim: "Disabling JUCE's fallback (NIT 2) would result in dropped notes, which is worse UX than an imperfect steal in a 25-note catastrophic overflow." In the realistic worst case (16-note voiceCap, 8 reserve voices, all 24 mid-fade), the 25th note IS rare + audibly indistinguishable between L7(b)-picked victim and JUCE-picked victim — both algorithms steal "the oldest" within their respective populations. Aligns with Jeff's L7(b) lock at Task 0 ExitPlanMode: "We never want to drop a new note." |
| NIT 3: Declaration-order subtle dependency in VibeVoice header at [VibePlayerDSP.h:349-357](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.h:349) — `mForwardSrc` / `mReverseSrc` MUST precede `mForwardResamp` / `mReverseResamp` because resampler member-init expressions take `&mForwardSrc` / `&mReverseSrc`; a drive-by alphabetize or accident-of-refactor reorder would silently produce a use-of-uninitialized-member at VibeVoice construction and segfault on the first note-on. No compiler warning would catch the reorder. | **Fixed in fix-up `36fe7fb`.** 8-line strict warning comment added in the source-class declaration block above `mForwardSrc` documenting the declaration-order constraint + the segfault hazard + the explicit "Do NOT reorder" directive. Jeff verbatim: "We don't want anyone alphabetizing the header and causing a segfault." |
| NIT 4: `mAdsrOverridden` + `mPreStealAdsrParams` survive `releaseResources()`. Existing source comment at cpp:507-509 is accurate but terse. | **Reframed as accepted design.** Verbatim: "NIT 4 and 5 are purely academic memory hygiene; startNote state reconciliation handles it safely." The state persists harmlessly across `releaseResources` because the next `startNote` is the natural restore point; `setAdsr` calls between fade-out-end and next-startNote route correctly through the `mAdsrOverridden`-guarded path in `setAdsr` itself. Behavior is deterministic + already documented in running-notes at Task 3 Section 2. |
| NIT 5: `mAdsrOverridden` cleared only in `startNote` — stays true forever if voice is stolen + faded + never re-triggered. Memory cost: one bool + one `juce::ADSR::Parameters` struct per voice. Already accounted for in per-voice RAM budget. | **Reframed as accepted design.** Same shape as NIT 4 — save/restore pair where if next `startNote` never fires, the saved state is never observed, dangling-true flag has zero observable effect. "Clear in startNote on next-note-on" is also slightly more defensive: if any future code path were to read `mAdsrOverridden` between fade-out-end + next-startNote, the flag would correctly report "yes, this voice's ADSR has been overridden" — which is true until the next startNote restores it. |
| NIT 6: Stale lifecycle comment at [VibePlayerDSP.h:251](Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.h:251) — read `// startNote -> find region -> create MemoryAudioSource + ResamplingAudioSource`. Post-Task-2 the lifecycle does NOT "create" anything per note — it re-points the fat sources. The "create" language survived from the pre-batch heap-alloc-per-note pattern and is now misleading. Task 6 grep sweep missed it because the stale comment text doesn't match any of the swept patterns. | **Fixed in fix-up `36fe7fb`.** Single-line comment rewrite: `//   startNote   -> find region -> re-point fat sources -> reset ADSR / filter / LFO`. |

#### Carry-forward contradictions

- **Carry-Forward Reference §1 (Render Engine Primitives) — VibePlayer voice-source architecture replaced.** Pre-QA-VoicePool the standing voice-source architecture documented at Carry-Forward Reference §1 (frozen 2026-05-07) was per-note `std::make_unique<juce::MemoryAudioSource>` + `std::make_unique<ReversedMemoryAudioSource>` + `std::make_unique<juce::ResamplingAudioSource>` allocated inside `VibeVoice::startNote` (3 heap allocations per note-on at the pre-batch VibePlayerDSP.cpp:581 / :583 / :607), plus a `std::vector<int> candidates` heap allocation per note in `VibeSampleManager::findRegion` at the pre-batch :415. Post-QA-VoicePool the architecture is fat-voice direct-member ownership: VibeVoice owns `VibeForwardMemoryAudioSource mForwardSrc` + `ReversedMemoryAudioSource mReverseSrc` + `juce::ResamplingAudioSource mForwardResamp { &mForwardSrc, false, 2 }` + `juce::ResamplingAudioSource mReverseResamp { &mReverseSrc, false, 2 }` permanently as direct members; `startNote` re-points the active-source / active-resampler pointer pair via `setBuffer` + `flushBuffers` + `setResamplingRatio`; `findRegion` uses a `std::array<int, 32>` stack-alloc with bounded-write overflow handling. The physical voice pool is over-provisioned `kMaxVoices = 24` (was 16) with `kLogicalCap = 16` reserve-voice landing zone for stolen-voice ADSR-quick-release fade-out overflow. Lock-free atomic occupancy (`std::atomic<bool> mIsActive` per voice) + `std::array<VibeVoice*, kMaxVoices> mVoices` direct pointer cache strip all hot-path `dynamic_cast<VibeVoice*>` from the audio thread. Voice stealing is 3-tier hybrid (release > noteOff-queued-or-key-released > key-down-PROTECTED with oldest-within-tier) plus a look-ahead noteOff pre-scan at top of `VibeSynth::renderNextBlock` that flips a transient `mNoteOffQueued` flag on voices whose noteOffs are pending this block, preserving sample-accurate MIDI timing. Per `feedback_closed_batch_carryforward_via_forks.md` Carry-Forward Reference is FROZEN; contradiction recorded here in the Work Log instead.

### 2026-05-27 14:00 PT — QA-SfzGroup — SFZ `<group>` opcode-inheritance state machine + Aria/sfizz RR loss investigation + keyswitching + UI labels + targeted sfizz atomic patch

**Bucket:** Players, Cross-cutting Infrastructure

#### Done

QA-SfzGroup is the close-spawned detour from QA-VoicePool FND-2 (per §9 thirty-eighth Forks entry) — slotted as the very next batch after QA-VoicePool per Jeff's verbatim "very next batch after we close QA-VoicePool" lock at QA-VoicePool Task 4 surface.  Original scope (per §5 entry + §9 thirty-eighth Forks entry, ~4-6 hr estimate): fix the broken `<group>` opcode-inheritance state machine in `VibeSampleManager::parseSFZ` (Track 1) + investigate the parallel sfizz code path for the same audible RR-loss symptom across BaySickRustyDrums + BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses (Track 2).  What actually shipped is a strict superset: parser-fix surface expanded TWICE mid-batch (12 opcodes → 18 with Sub-L/M/N/O keyswitching scope-expansion at Task 2 mid-verify; 18 → 19 with Sub-P/Q UI-discoverability scope-expansion at Task 2 verify session) PLUS Sub-A's investigation-only lock for Track 2 was AMENDED at Task 3 via Sub-R/S to allow a targeted vendored-sfizz atomic patch (single-symbol type change, bounded).  Net architectural change: BaySickPlayer's hand-rolled SFZ parser now implements the full 4-level cascading inheritance from the SFZ v1 spec (`<global>` → `<master>` → `<group>` → `<region>`) via a function-local helper struct with COPY-at-scope-enter accumulators; 19-opcode coverage includes the 6-opcode keyswitching engine (`sw_lokey` / `sw_hikey` / `sw_last` / `sw_down` / `sw_up` / `sw_default`) + `sw_label` readToEOL opcode; VibePlayerProcessor::processBlock has a Sub-M pre-scan loop that strips keyswitch events from the MIDI buffer before mSynth.renderNextBlock so VibeSynth never sees keyswitches; piano keyboards on Layer/Bass/Drum/Clip pages render keyswitch keys with amber-highlight + bold dark-amber `sw_label` text + tooltip surfacing; vendored sfizz `Layer.h:151` `sequenceCounter_` converted to `std::atomic<int>` with explicit `fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_relaxed)` at both call sites as defense-in-depth (Sub-R/S patch did NOT resolve the bit-crusher MT-mode symptom on Rusty cymbals/hi-hats discovered mid-Task-3 — diagnostic miss owned, actual race source is elsewhere in sfizz, routed to follow-up batch).  Originally Sub-A locked Track 2 as investigation-only with sfizz-side fixes routed to a NEW dedicated batch; the Sub-R amendment surgically opened that lock for the single-symbol type change while preserving Sub-A's spirit (no full sfizz state-machine patching mixed with parser work).  Sub-P locked the keyswitch-label UI to BaySickPlayer-driven piano rolls only (Layer/Bass/Drum/Clip tabs); the 3 sfizz-driven engines (BaySickRustyDrums + BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses) get UI labels in the follow-up batch (Sub-T routing decision).

**Layer 1 — Sub-K(c) helper-struct parser refactor (Task 2 commit `5cd5f17`):** the pre-batch broken early-return `if (!inRegion) continue;` at the pre-batch `Source/VibePlayer/VibePlayerDSP.cpp:148` replaced with a function-local `enum class Scope { None, Global, Master, Group, Region }` + `SfzParseState` struct carrying 4 cascading-default `VibeRegion` accumulators (`globalDefaults` / `masterDefaults` / `groupDefaults` + `current`) + 6 enter-scope methods (`enterGlobal` / `enterMaster` / `enterGroup` / `enterRegion` / `enterControl` / `exitOther`) + `tryFlushRegion` + `currentTarget()` returning the right `VibeRegion*` by scope; per-line dispatch becomes `auto* t = state.currentTarget(); if (! t) continue;` and all opcode blocks write to `*t`.  4-level inheritance realized by COPY at scope-enter time: `enterMaster()` copies `globalDefaults → masterDefaults`; `enterGroup()` copies `masterDefaults → groupDefaults`; `enterRegion()` copies `groupDefaults → current` as the region's baseline before per-region opcode overrides.  `<control>` block stays orthogonal (default_path scope only, scope=None + inControl=true).  Tuba-KS.sfz's 4 staccato `<group>` blocks each declaring `seq_length=4` + `seq_position=1..4` now correctly cascade into every `<region>` they parent — the §9 thirty-eighth Forks entry's parser-bug diagnosis is resolved by construction.

**Layer 2 — Keyswitching (Task 2 commit `5cd5f17`, Sub-L/M/N/O):** 6 new opcodes added (Sub-B amended 12 → 18) — `sw_lokey` / `sw_hikey` / `sw_last` / `sw_down` / `sw_up` / `sw_default`.  VibeRegion gained 6 keyswitch fields.  SfzParseState gained 4 explicit-tracking fields for Sub-O scope-priority init (`explicitGlobalSwDefault` / `explicitMasterSwDefault` / `explicitGroupSwDefault` / `firstRegionSwDefault`, first-wins per scope) + `captureExplicitSwDefault(int)` + `getInitialSwLast() const` resolving Global > Master > Group > first-Region priority chain.  End-of-parseSFZ populates `mIsKeyswitch[128]` from union of `swLokey..swHikey` ranges across all loaded regions + inits `mActiveSwLast` via scope-priority chain.  VibeSampleManager gains `mActiveSwLast` + `mSwDownHeld[128]` + `mIsKeyswitch[128]` state + `isKeyswitchNote(int) const noexcept` / `handleKeyswitchNoteOn(int) noexcept` / `handleKeyswitchNoteOff(int) noexcept` / `resetKeyswitchState() noexcept` methods.  `clear()` calls `resetKeyswitchState()` for lifecycle reset.  `findRegion`'s candidate loop gains 3 keyswitch filter conditions BEFORE existing note/velocity/articulationGroup checks (`r.swLast != -1 && mActiveSwLast != r.swLast` / `r.swDown != -1 && !mSwDownHeld[r.swDown]` / `r.swUp != -1 && mSwDownHeld[r.swUp]`).  VibePlayerProcessor::processBlock gains a Sub-M pre-scan loop after audition events + before `mSynth.renderNextBlock` that walks the MIDI buffer, updates manager state via `handleKeyswitchNoteOn` / `handleKeyswitchNoteOff` + STRIPS the event from the buffer for any note-on/off where `mgr.isKeyswitchNote(note)` is true (scratch buffer `mKeyswitchFilteredMidi` member added to avoid per-block allocation).  VibeSynth only ever sees playable notes — no voice-stealing trigger from keyswitch presses + no wasted findFreeVoice cycles.  **Natural divide-by-zero fix at findRegion:520:** the latent `counter = (counter % rrTotal) + 1` crash when `rrTotal=0` (mixed sustain/staccato candidate pool with only some candidates RR-marked, discovered mid-Task-2 verify as a real pre-existing bug exposed by the parser fix populating staccato regions correctly) is structurally resolved by keyswitching isolating sustain vs staccato candidates BEFORE the RR logic runs — mixed pool can't form, no defensive math added.

**Layer 3 — UI discoverability (Task 2 commit `5cd5f17`, Sub-P/Q):** 19th opcode `sw_label` (readToEOL=true since values contain spaces like `sw_label="C6 Sustain"` / `"C#6 Staccato"`; Sub-B amended 18 → 19).  VibeRegion gains `juce::String swLabel`.  VibeSampleManager gains `std::array<juce::String, 128> mKeyswitchLabels` + `getKeyswitchLabel(int) const noexcept` public method + populates `mKeyswitchLabels` from each region's `(swLast/swDown/swUp, swLabel)` pair (first-wins per keyswitch note).  PianoRollConnection gains `std::function<juce::String(int)> keyswitchLabelProvider` field.  PianoRollPage::registerEngine wires `roll->setKeyswitchLabelProvider(...)` if set.  PianoKeyboard + PianoRollContainer gain `setKeyswitchLabelProvider` method.  PianoKeyboard::paint gains a keyswitch-key branch at the TOP of the per-note loop (full-width amber background `Colour(0xffe8c060)` resting + `Colour(0xfff5d690)` brighter when previewed + bold dark-amber label text sized to the row height).  PianoKeyboard::getTooltip checks the keyswitch provider first (priority over noteLabelProvider) so hover surfaces the label.  4 closures wired in StandaloneEditor.cpp at Layer/Bass/Drum/Clip piano-roll registration sites (closure does `dynamic_cast<VibePlayerProcessor*>` guard + returns `v->getSynth().getManager().getKeyswitchLabel(n)` on success / empty on failure); non-BaySickPlayer engines (Harmless / BaySickSynth / BaySickBass) cast-fail → empty → no amber rendering → existing keyboard behavior preserved unchanged.

**Layer 4 — Targeted vendored-sfizz atomic patch (Task 3 commit `821b561`, Sub-R amendment + Sub-S memory-order lock):** `libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Layer.h:11` added `#include <atomic>`; `Layer.h:151` `int sequenceCounter_ { 0 };` → `std::atomic<int> sequenceCounter_ { 0 };` + 7-line QA-SfzGroup local-patch comment.  `Layer.cpp:63` (registerNoteOn) + `:191` (registerCC) both call sites use `const int counter = sequenceCounter_.fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_relaxed);` then modulo check against the `counter` local — explicit fetch_add NOT operator++ since operator++ on std::atomic defaults to seq_cst.  Patch is semantically correct (plain-int RMW under MT is UB per C++ spec; atomic with relaxed is the right pick for a monotonic counter not synchronizing other state) + ships as defense-in-depth, BUT empirical verification post-clean-rebuild (deleted `build/sfizz_build/` + rebuilt) confirmed the bit-crusher MT-mode symptom on Rusty cymbals/hi-hats UNCHANGED — `sequenceCounter_` race hypothesis was wrong, actual race source is elsewhere in sfizz (candidates not investigated: voice allocation in `Synth::Impl::startVoice` at Synth.cpp:1300, voice manager state, sample buffer concurrent access in Voice.cpp / RegionStateful.cpp, polyphony manager).  Per Jeff's verbatim "We are going with Option (b): Keep the atomic patch + route the deep investigation to the follow-up batch", the actual bit-crusher fix routes to the follow-up batch + the atomic patch stays in tree.

#### Found along the way

1. **Latent findRegion:520 div-by-zero crash on first pitched-keypress post-Task-2-initial-edit** — pre-batch parser dropped every `<group>`-scoped opcode → no region ever had `roundRobinTotal > 0` → modulo against `roundRobinTotal` at the pre-batch :520 was structurally unreachable.  Track 1 parser fix correctly populated RR markers on staccato regions while sustain stayed RR-less → mixed candidate pool → `candidates[0]` was sustain with `roundRobinTotal=0` → div-by-zero.  **Resolved STRUCTURALLY by Sub-L/M/N/O keyswitching (Layer 2)** — no defensive math in findRegion.

2. **Octave-naming convention clarification (Task 2 verify session, no source change)** — SFZ file uses IPN (C4 = MIDI 60); BaySickDAW UI uses FL Studio convention (C5 = MIDI 60) per `Source/Standalone/DrumKitGrid.cpp:17`.  Tuba-KS's `sw_default=c6` (MIDI 84) displays in the app as C7 — two-octave offset.  Documenting for future verify scripts to use FL-convention notation when describing BaySickPlayer keyswitches.

3. **MAJOR Track 3 inventory finding (Task 1)** — BaySickRustyDrums has a wrapper-synthesis loader path (`buildOutputRoutedSfzWrapper` at `BaySickRustyDrumsProcessor.cpp:623`) that REWRITES SFZ content + injects `output=N` into every `<master>`/`<group>` line before handing to sfizz via `loadSfzString`.  BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses use plain `loadSfzFile`.  Asymmetry surfaced as suspect in Task 1 inventory but Task 3 investigation confirmed the wrapper is opcode-preserving + sfizz inheritance works correctly → **NOT a bug source**.  Documented for future reference; rules out one of the four Task-1 triage-matrix hypotheses.

4. **Bit-crusher MT regression on Rusty cymbals/hi-hats (Task 3, FIXED ATTEMPTED + NOT RESOLVED + ROUTED)** — surfaced mid-Task-3 live testing.  Symptom: MT-only (clean under MT-off), scales with RR variant count (cymbals/hi-hats with 6-8 RR slots affected; kick/snare with fewer slots clean), single-hit reproducible.  Jeff escalated via the principle "we do not defer catastrophic bugs that we are actively looking at, especially when they directly relate to the batch's core mandate (SFZ RR behavior)" — triggered Sub-R/S amendment + targeted atomic patch (Layer 4).  Patch removes a real data race (plain-int RMW under MT is UB) but did NOT resolve the symptom.  Routed to follow-up batch (Sub-T item 3) for the deep-investigation work.

5. **Guitars/Basses RR-loss observed in live testing (Task 3, NOT RESOLVED + ROUTED)** — Jeff reported NO RR variation audible on those engines despite content having RR + sfizz handling inheritance correctly.  Likely-root-cause hypothesis: discoverability blocker (Sub-P locked sfizz-engine keyswitch labels OUT of QA-SfzGroup; Jeff couldn't engage articulations or hit at consistent velocity to test RR meaningfully).  Routed to follow-up batch (Sub-T item 2) where the discoverability fix lands first (Sub-T item 1) — if RR audible post-discoverability-fix, item 2 is closed; if not, deeper sfizz dive.

6. **Process meta-finding — NEW Main Plan §0 Rule 5 landed at QA-SfzGroup Task 0** — "Sub-spec calls discovered during planning surface via chat BEFORE landing in the plan body."  Adopted 2026-05-26 mid-QA-SfzGroup plan-mode after recurring violations across QA-VoicePool / QA-InsertMaps / QA-SfzGroup plans where new sub-spec calls were pre-picked + baked into task bodies + only listed as table rows in the "Sub-spec calls surfaced for ExitPlanMode" section.  Cross-refs `feedback_dont_make_unilateral_spec_calls.md` plan-mode section + `Files For Claude/batch_session_boilerplate.md` step 5b + standing-rules section.  Rule lands in the SAME COMMIT as the QA-SfzGroup Task 0 batch-open (`a92f55a`) so future plan-mode sessions reading Main Plan §0 see it before drafting any plan body.

#### Carry-Forward contradictions

None architectural.  Carry-Forward §1 (Render Engine Primitives) doesn't document SFZ parser behavior either way (this was bug-fix territory not architectural-primitive change).  Carry-Forward §2 (Lock-Free + Lifecycle Primitives) doesn't document sfizz's internal threading model; the Sub-R/S atomic patch adds memory-order discipline to a vendored library's internal counter, supplementing Carry-Forward's MT primitive set without contradicting it.

#### Routing to follow-up batch

Three findings routed to a NEW dedicated batch per Sub-T (b) + Jeff's verbatim slot lock "we are gonna route that batch to immediately after this one so that we are all done with the sfz aria stuff all in 'one' go":

- **Item 1 — Keyswitch label discoverability for sfizz-driven engines** (BaySickRustyDrums + BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses).  Extend Sub-P/Q's amber-highlight + label rendering to those engines' piano keyboards.  Requires extracting keyswitch labels from sfizz's parsed Region/Layer data structures (the parsed sw_label opcode + sw_lokey..sw_hikey range data accessible via sfizz's existing public API).  Wire the existing `PianoRollConnection::keyswitchLabelProvider` field (already added in Task 2 at PianoRoll.h) at the BaySickRustyDrums + BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses piano-roll registration sites in StandaloneEditor.cpp with closures that pull labels from each engine's mSfizz instance.  Estimated effort medium ~2-3 hr.

- **Item 2 — Guitars/Basses RR-loss diagnosis.**  Most likely resolved by item 1 (Jeff can engage articulations + test at consistent velocity once labels visible).  If RR still doesn't audibly cycle post-discoverability-fix, dive deeper into sfizz `Default::sequence` value semantics + sample-data inspection + actual loaded-Region inheritance state.  Estimated effort small ~1-2 hr if resolved by item 1, medium ~3-4 hr if separate fix needed.

- **Item 3 — Bit-crusher MT race fix.**  Catastrophic bug; atomic patch in `libs/sfizz/Layer.{h,cpp}` stays in tree as defense-in-depth but does NOT resolve the symptom.  Actual root cause needs file-logging instrumentation (DBG / OutputDebugString invisible in Release without DebugView; message-thread file-output is the viable signal channel) + per-voice / per-Layer / per-noteOn state capture during a multi-voice cymbal hit + identify racing state in voice allocation / dispatch / sample buffer / polyphony manager / RegionStateful.  Estimated effort medium-large ~3-6 hr investigation + ~1-2 hr fix.

**Total estimated follow-up batch effort: ~6-12 hours.  Slot per Sub-T: immediately after QA-SfzGroup close, before QA-EngineApvts.**

Same per-engine public accessor shape as Task 2A on [BaySickGuitarsProcessor.{h,cpp}](Source/BaySickGuitars/BaySickGuitarsProcessor.h) + [BaySickBassesProcessor.{h,cpp}](Source/BaySickBasses/BaySickBassesProcessor.h) — null-guard + iterate + match + return.  StandaloneEditor.cpp gains the 2 `conn.keyswitchLabelProvider` closures at the `EngineKind::BaySickGuitars` branch `:7980` and `EngineKind::BaySickBasses` branch `:7993` inside `registerInstSourcePianoRoll(InstPage*)`.  5 files, +54 / -0.  All 3 sfizz-driven engines now expose the same `getKeyswitchLabel(int)` interface, mirroring the existing 3-way `setOnAnyStateChange` + `getUndoManager` accessor parity at the same public-cluster position.  Item 1 closes — amber keyswitch labels visible on BaySickRustyDrums + BaySickGuitars + BaySickBasses piano rolls per the QA-SfzGroup Task 2 Tuba-KS UX reference.

**Layer 3 — Item 2 Branch A closure (verify-only) + Sub-E mid-batch Aria-CC=64 emulation expansion (Task 3 close commit `f477e39`):** Item 2 (Guitars/Basses round-robin loss) closes Branch A per the plan body's verify-then-diag structure — kit content limitation, NOT a code bug.  Item 2 was diagnosed via Sub-D=(c) diagnostic instrumentation at libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Synth.cpp + Layer.cpp (post-load Region dump at end of `finalizeSfzLoad` + per-call note-on dispatch trace inside `Layer::registerNoteOn` `if (keyOk)` block; file-logger writing to `Documents/BaySickDAW/qa-sfizz-task3-trace.log` per `feedback_follow_existing_folder_convention.md`); Trace #1 (8029 lines) verified sfizz RR cycling working correctly with seq_pos cycling cleanly 1→2→3→4→1 across 19 key presses; Sub-D refinement (sample path + velocity range to LOAD dump + NOTEON-FIRE trace) confirmed Trace #2 (post-refinement) showed 15 fires across 4 unique samples in pattern rr1→rr2→rr3→rr4 repeating cleanly through ~3.75 full cycles; Jeff's perceptual test on raw .wav files outside BaySickDAW (Windows Media Player + Audacity) confirmed the 4 Karoryfer RR variants sound identical or near-identical despite being distinct files with different MD5 hashes + file sizes.  sfizz parser + dispatch + RR cycling all verified working correctly; Karoryfer RR variants are perceptually too similar to produce audible variation under default kit settings.  Sub-D=(c) instrumentation stripped at Task 3 close via `git restore --source=HEAD -- libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Synth.cpp libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Layer.cpp` (Rule 4 catalog Disposition; the strip itself produces ZERO commit-visible diff for the 2 sfizz files).

**Sub-E mid-batch scope expansion (folded into Task 3 close `f477e39`):** while investigating the Aria-player-settings angle on Item 2, surfaced a separate side-finding correlating with a long-standing perception ("the drum kit has always sounded quiet or 'thin' to me").  Root cause identified: the K-5 fix (2026-05-05) defaults all APVTS CC params to 0 to match sfizz's "unset CC = 0" SFZ-spec internal convention, but Karoryfer kits are designed for Aria hosts which default CCs to 64 (MIDI center); Karoryfer `<master>`/`<group>` blocks gate on CC ranges expecting CC=64 default (Big Rusty Drums: 88 masters gate on CC4 hi-hat position + 72 on CC25 + 64 on CC28; Black&Green Guitars: unison masters gate on `locc100=1`, tailpiece masters on `locc118=64`, feedback masters on `locc29=1`; Black&Blue Basses similar).  Sub-E=(a)+(i) lock Jeff verbatim 2026-05-28: "I am not deferring this... The K-5 KVR fix makes sense for generic SFZ, but Rusty, Guitars, and Basses are Aria hosts. They need to emulate the Aria environment (CC=64) so the unison layers and hi-hats fire properly by default."  Sub-E source landed across all 3 sfizz-driven engine processors (9 Edits total, 3 per engine: `getKitDefaultCc` fallback `return 0` → `return 64` + `createLayout` AudioParameterInt default `0` → `64` + `loadKit` reset baseline `0.0f` → `64.0f`).  All 9 sites have updated comments explaining the Aria-host-convention rationale + cross-referencing the K-5/L-5 history.  Kit-author `set_cc<N>=<int>` directives still override during loadKit (so if a kit explicitly sets CC100=0, that wins over the 64 baseline).  Sub-E mechanism correction journey documented as audit trail: I initially claimed Sub-E would "add masters firing → fuller sound"; Jeff DBFS-tested + reported no audible level change ("I wouldn't say it sounds louder, and dbfs proves that, but any more action on this is chasing a dragon"); reading corrected — Karoryfer kits author `<master>` blocks in OPPOSITE-direction CC ranges (e.g., main Twang `hicc118=63` ≤ 63 + tailpiece Twang `locc118=64` ≥ 64, mutually exclusive ranges that SELECT WHICH variant fires, not whether an additional variant fires); Sub-E SWAPS which variant the user hears by default (main → tailpiece on this example), it doesn't ADD a tailpiece-on-top-of-main layer.  Sub-E's CORE purpose (Aria-host convention emulation) stands separate from the loudness hypothesis — the kit author DESIGNED `<master>` selection around Aria's CC=64 default, so which articulation variant Aria users hear by default IS the author's intended default; under K-5's CC=0, users hear a DIFFERENT default articulation variant than Aria-authored kits intend.  Jeff verbatim on keep-vs-revert: "You found an issue with how you set something up, it didn't fix an issue you knew it wouldn't, and then you said lets remove the fix for the other issue because it didn't fix this?"  Sub-E stays.  "Thin sound" perception remains a real issue but is NOT fixable via blanket CC default change — it's a per-kit / per-CC user-control surface, not a code default; future investigation (out-of-scope for QA-Sfizz) would be per-kit articulation-engagement defaults.

**Layer 4 — Item 3 Sub-G=(a) narrow audio-entry SpinLock attempt (Task 5 source landing, REVERTED FAILED):** Sub-F=(e) Engine Boundary & Instance Trace at Task 4 (Jeff-locked custom diagnostic option overriding the 4 mechanical options A/B/C/D I proposed; Jeff verbatim "We need to pull back a layer before we dive into sfizz's internal voice architecture... The symptoms strongly suggest that VibeThreadPool might be parallelizing at the voice/note level and throwing multiple threads at the exact same sfz::Sfizz instance concurrently") captured 1804 `RUSTY-PROCESS-STRIPS` entries across 9 distinct thread IDs all hitting `mSfizz=000001D07C65AF30` + 1795 `SFIZZ-RENDER-BLOCK` entries on the same 9 thread IDs hitting `synth=000001D0441121F0`.  I initially interpreted the 9-thread-IDs-on-one-mSfizz pattern as proof of concurrent worker-pool task-stealing into the same `sfz::Sfizz` instance ("Bullseye" lock — diagnostic misread owned in this entry).  Sub-G=(a) `juce::SpinLock mSfizzAccessLock` + `ScopedLockType lk` added at audio-thread entry on all 3 sfizz-driven engine processors (Rusty: `processStrips` + `processBlock`; Guitars + Basses: `processBlock`).  Jeff verified post-build: bit-crusher PERSISTED on Rusty MT-on; ruled out concurrent renderBlock hypothesis.  Reverted via `git restore --source=HEAD -- <6 engine processor files>`.

**Layer 5 — Item 3 Sub-I=(c) widened leaf-node SpinLock attempt (Task 5 follow-up source landing, REVERTED FAILED):** Sub-I=(f)+(c) lock Jeff verbatim: "apply a temporary, broad lock that synchronizes PluginProcessor::processBlock WITH the parameterChanged APVTS listener (and any other asynchronous paths like sendCc or MIDI message injection).  Let's see if fencing off the entire plugin instance from asynchronous state mutation cures the bit-crusher."  Reentrancy analysis surfaced 3 deadlock paths in broad coverage (Rusty's `processBlock` calls `processStrips` internally; `sendCc` writes APVTS synchronously firing `parameterChanged`; `loadKit` writes ~512 APVTS CC params via `setValueNotifyingHost` in the Sub-E reset loop); Jeff-approved leaf-node-locking matrix avoided all three (LOCK at `processStrips` Rusty / `processBlock` Guitars+Basses / `parameterChanged` all 3 / `getNumActiveVoices` + `getKeyswitchLabel` const accessors with `mutable` lock; SKIP at `processBlock` Rusty / `sendCc` / `loadKit`).  6 files / +100 / -0 pure additive landed in working tree.  Jeff verified post-build: "It persists and confirmed in st it still works fine."  Bit-crusher persisted on Rusty MT-on; ST/MT-off confirmed clean; async APVTS mutation hypothesis FALSIFIED — race is NOT at the engine/sfizz state level on either the audio-entry surface or any APVTS leaf path.  Reverted via `git restore --source=HEAD -- <6 engine processor files>`.

**Layer 6 — Item 3 Sub-K=(custom) Serial Fallback fix (Task 5 follow-up commit `0e57fc5`, closes Item 3):** Jeff verbatim on the architectural-batch scoping discipline: "Candidate A is a fantastic architectural catch.  The mMultiOutScratch read-write race across block boundaries perfectly explains why this only affects the multi-out Rusty engine, why the FX rack InsertNodes are catching torn samples on long sustains, and why both lock attempts failed.  However, building a barrier-based synchronization system for the dispatcher is way out of scope for QA-Sfizz.  We need to stabilize the audio and close this batch.  Sub-K: Custom Option (e) Serial Fallback.  We are bypassing the bug for now.  Modify the dispatch logic to route BaySickRustyDrums, BaySickGuitars, and BaySickBasses OUT of the MT path.  Force them to render strictly on the serial path."  Critical architectural constraint discovered at Sub-L planning: the pre-existing "MT-off" toggle (`gMultiThreadedEngineEnabled`) does NOT route through a separate serial code path — that was deleted at QA-Ef 2026-05-21 ("the serial render path was deleted and the dispatcher became the single, unconditional render path"); MT-off just parks the worker threads; the audio thread drains the ENTIRE graph itself via `runUntilOrTimeout`.  Per-engine serial fallback therefore requires PER-TASK audio-thread affinity within the unified dispatcher, not routing to a non-existent separate code path.  Sub-L=(impl-1) per-task `bool mAudioThreadOnly` flag on `RenderTask` + dual-queue MPSC infrastructure in `VibeThreadPool` (Jeff lock: "This is the correct, standard lock-free pattern for thread-pinning.  Option 2 (worker requeue) would cause catastrophic cache trashing and priority inversion spinning.  Option 3 breaks the DAG model.").  Sub-M=(eng-b) `InstStripTask`'s `mAudioThreadOnly` flag set at engine-swap time on the message thread inside `VibeSynthProcessor::registerInstEngine` via `dynamic_cast<BaySickGuitarsProcessor*>` or `dynamic_cast<BaySickBassesProcessor*>` checks before `registerTask` (Jeff lock: "Using RTTI inside the audio thread's per-block dispatch loop is a massive anti-pattern that can cause non-deterministic execution times.  Computing the flag strictly on the UI/Message thread during an engine swap guarantees zero overhead in the audio hot path.").  Sub-K source landing (5 files, ~+125 / -0 pure additive): [RenderTask.h](Source/Engine/RenderTask.h) `bool mAudioThreadOnly = false;` field with Sub-K/Sub-L rationale block (plain bool not atomic since set on message thread at task construction or engine-swap time, never from the audio thread); [VibeThreadPool.cpp](Source/Engine/VibeThreadPool.cpp) second `moodycamel::ConcurrentQueue<RenderTask*> audioThreadQueue` in `Impl` + `submit()` routes by `task->mAudioThreadOnly` (audio queue skips worker wake; worker queue keeps existing wake behavior) + `runUntil` + `runUntilOrTimeout` priority-pop `audioThreadQueue.try_dequeue` ahead of the existing `tryPop()` worker queue + `clearQueues` drains both; [RustyDrumsProducerTask.cpp](Source/Engine/Tasks/RustyDrumsProducerTask.cpp) + [RustyInsertTask.cpp](Source/Engine/Tasks/RustyInsertTask.cpp) constructor sets `mAudioThreadOnly = true;` natively (covers Rusty's 1 producer + 13 strip tasks for multi-out); [PluginProcessor.cpp](Source/PluginProcessor.cpp) `registerInstEngine` does `dynamic_cast<BaySickGuitarsProcessor*>(eng) != nullptr || dynamic_cast<BaySickBassesProcessor*>(eng) != nullptr` and sets `task->mAudioThreadOnly = true` before `mRenderDispatcher.registerTask(task.get())`.  Jeff verify 2026-05-28: "Pass" — Sub-K cures the bit-crusher on the MT-on 6-cymbal crash test; no regressions on Guitars/Basses (defensive pinning; content doesn't exhibit the Rusty multi-out race trigger but precautionary serialization costs nothing audible) / Drums/Layers/Vox/non-sfizz engines (worker-eligible pool unchanged for everything except the 4 sfizz task families) / MT-off paths (audio-thread pinning is a no-op when only one thread enters the audio path).

I do not want to lose these specific failure modes to chat history.").  Per `feedback_canonical_structure_no_eliding.md` ethos — capture full scope while context is fresh, don't elide for terseness.

#### Found along the way

1. **FND-1 (Phase 1 research) — sfizz public-API gap for keyswitch labels.**  sfizz exposes `getKeyLabels()` ([Synth.h:707](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Synth.h:707)) and `getCCLabels()` ([Synth.h:713](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/Synth.h:713)) but no public keyswitch-label accessor.  Internal data lives at [SynthPrivate.h:288](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/SynthPrivate.h:288) `std::vector<NoteNamePair> keyswitchLabels_;` + map at `:289`.  Routed to SC-4 sub-spec call mid-Phase-1-research; resolved (a) (extend SC-3=(b) authorization).

2. **FND-2 (Task 1 inventory) — Item 3 instrumentation file landscape expansion.**  The plan-body's abstract "polyphony manager" reference resolves to THREE separate sfizz files ([PolyphonyGroup.{h,cpp}](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/PolyphonyGroup.h) + [VoiceManager.{h,cpp}](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/VoiceManager.h) + [VoiceStealing.{h,cpp}](libs/sfizz/src/sfizz/VoiceStealing.h)) plus the orchestrator `Synth::Impl::startVoice` at Synth.cpp:1300 + per-voice `Voice::startVoice` at Voice.cpp:408 + Region.cpp / RegionSet.cpp / RegionStateful.cpp + Layer.cpp (already touched by QA-SfzGroup Sub-R/S).  NOT a new spec call — the §5 entry's Item 3 scope "dig into sfizz's MT execution model: worker pool / lock-free queues / shared state" already covers any of these, just a finding that expands Task 4's instrumentation candidate list under the unchanged SC-3=(b) bounded-patch envelope.

3. **FND-3 (Task 3 Trace #1 analysis) — Karoryfer Black&Green Guitars + Black&Blue Basses RR is fully working.**  Cross-tab of `isSwitchedOn=1` lines by `(counter, seq_pos)` showed exactly 4 layers fire per cycle position with seq_pos cycling cleanly 1→2→3→4→1 across 19 key presses.  Sample files (twang_f3_f_rr1..4.wav) verified distinct on disk via `md5sum` (4 unique hashes + 4 distinct file sizes 702K/630K/652K/822K).  Karoryfer kit content DOES declare `seq_length=4` + `seq_position=1..4` correctly across `Programs/modules/maps_green/ord.sfz`.  sfizz parser + dispatch + cycling all correct.

4. **FND-4 (Task 3 Trace #2 analysis) — Karoryfer RR variants are perceptually too similar to produce audible variation.**  Jeff's perceptual test on raw .wav files outside BaySickDAW (Windows Media Player + Audacity) confirmed: the 4 sample files sound identical or near-identical despite being distinct files.  Item 2 closes Branch A — kit content limitation, NOT a code bug.

5. **FND-5 (Task 3 mid-investigation) — Aria-host CC=64 default convention drift.**  Karoryfer-authored sfizz kit content for all 3 engine families (Big Rusty Drums + Black&Green Guitars + Black&Blue Basses) gates `<master>` blocks on CC ranges expecting CC=64 default (the Aria host convention) but BaySickDAW's K-5 fix from 2026-05-05 defaults APVTS CCs to 0 (the SFZ spec's "unset CC" convention) — kit author DESIGNED `<master>` selection around Aria's CC=64 default, so which articulation variant Aria users hear by default IS the author's intended default.  Routed via Sub-E in-batch fold (NOT deferred per Jeff "I am not deferring this") + Sub-E mechanism correction journey when DBFS A/B refuted my initial "fuller sound" hypothesis but Sub-E's CORE Aria-emulation purpose stood separate.

6. **FND-6 (Task 4 Sub-F=(e) trace MISREAD owned) — entry-only thread-ID counts are work-stealing churn, NOT concurrent renderBlock overlap.**  The Sub-F=(e) trace captured ENTRY-only thread IDs (no exit timestamps).  9 distinct thread IDs across 1804 calls over a 6-second test is fully consistent with WORK-STEALING task-pool affinity churn (JUCE/VibeThreadPool rotates worker handling consecutive blocks; non-deterministic which worker gets a given block, but only ONE worker per block).  The trace did NOT prove overlap.  I called it "Bullseye" evidence at Sub-G=(a) lock; the corrected reading at Task 5 post-Sub-G-fail ruled out concurrent renderBlock execution.  Lesson learned: future trace instrumentation MUST capture entry+exit timestamps to prove concurrent vs sequential overlap definitively.

7. **FND-7 (Task 5 Sub-G + Sub-I empirical rule-outs) — race lives downstream of engine-level audio path.**  Both Sub-G=(a) narrow audio-entry SpinLock AND Sub-I=(c) widened leaf-node SpinLock failed to cure the bit-crusher symptom on Rusty MT-on (ST/MT-off confirmed clean throughout).  Concurrent renderBlock on same `mSfizz` instance RULED OUT.  Async APVTS mutation racing with audio render RULED OUT.  Race must live in a path that bypasses BOTH the audio-entry SpinLock AND the parameterChanged SpinLock — i.e., DOWNSTREAM of `processStrips` OR INSIDE sfizz via internal threading.

8. **FND-8 (Task 5 follow-up Candidate A surfacing) — mMultiOutScratch cross-block read-write race architectural hypothesis.**  Rusty's `processStrips` writes 13 strips into the engine-internal `mMultiOutScratch`.  PluginProcessor's per-strip `RustyInsertTask` family then reads each strip from that scratch via `getStripBuffer` — and runs in parallel on workers.  If the dispatcher doesn't BARRIER between blocks (i.e., block N+1's `RustyDrumsProducerTask` starts WRITING `mMultiOutScratch` before block N's 13 RustyInsertTasks have finished READING it), readers see torn samples → bit-crusher artifact at the InsertNode output stage.  Matches: why ONLY Rusty has the symptom (it's the only engine with multi-out scratch + per-strip InsertNode fan-out — Guitars/Basses are single stereo out, no scratch sharing); why ONLY long sustains audibly express it (cymbals hold the same scratch slot in use across many consecutive blocks; transient kicks finish before block N+1 fires); why Sub-G + Sub-I both failed (both lock the WRITE side; the InsertNode READ side is downstream — `RustyInsertTask::run()` reads `getStripBuffer` then runs its insert chain on workers without acquiring any sfizz-engine lock); why Sub-F=(e) trace showed 9 thread IDs on one `mSfizz` (sequential block dispatches on different workers — thread-affinity churn — NOT concurrent on the same block).

9. **FND-9 (Sub-K planning) — Critical architectural constraint at QA-Ef close.**  The pre-existing "MT-off" toggle (`gMultiThreadedEngineEnabled`) does NOT route through a separate serial code path — that was deleted at QA-Ef 2026-05-21 ("the serial render path was deleted and the dispatcher became the single, unconditional render path").  MT-off just parks the worker threads; the audio thread drains the ENTIRE graph itself via `runUntilOrTimeout`.  Per-engine serial fallback therefore requires PER-TASK audio-thread affinity within the unified dispatcher, not routing to a non-existent separate code path.  Drove Sub-L=(impl-1) dual-queue MPSC infrastructure pick over the conceptually-simpler "route to serial path" original wording.

10. **FND-10 (Sub-O Candidate-B expansion at Task 5 follow-up #3) — pre-commit scope check surfaced 3 additional bit-crusher root-cause hypotheses.**  Jeff surfaced 3 additional hypotheses he wanted captured before commit so they wouldn't be lost to chat history: Non-Atomic RR Voice Swapping (voice metadata + RR index racing across workers, shared state torn mid-block); False Sharing / Cache Line Invalidation (adjacent memory addresses bouncing cache lines between cores); Disk Streaming Engine Contention (non-thread-safe streaming queue cross-contaminating sample requests under high-RR I/O load).  All 3 are in the same general territory as Candidate B (sfizz internals); each is a distinct failure mode worth surfacing as its own sub-mechanism.  Routed via Sub-O = (int-a) to §5 QA-DispatcherAffinity scope + §9 fortieth Forks entry as B.1/B.2/B.3/B.4 sub-mechanisms.  Elegant convergence captured: Candidate B's per-engine-instance worker-affinity intervention naturally addresses ALL 4 sub-mechanisms (Candidate A's mMultiOutScratch race included) by single-threading sfizz access per engine instance.

#### Carry-forward contradictions

- **Carry-Forward Reference §1 (Render Engine Primitives) — `RenderTask::mAudioThreadOnly` + `VibeThreadPool::audioThreadQueue` are NEW post-QA-Ef MT primitives.**  Sub-K Serial Fallback adds per-task audio-thread affinity within the unified dispatcher via a dual-queue MPSC pattern (worker-eligible MPMC queue retains existing behavior; audio-thread-only MPSC queue only drained by `runUntilOrTimeout` with priority-pop ahead of the worker queue).  Carry-Forward §1 documents the MT path + `RenderGraphDispatcher::rebuildLinks()` + `dispatchBlock(const BlockContext&)` + per-engine MIDI buffers but doesn't document this affinity primitive.  Supplements §1 without contradicting; will retire entirely when QA-DispatcherAffinity lands the architectural fix.
- **Carry-Forward Reference §1 — `mMultiOutScratch` cross-block read-write race is a documented architectural correctness gap.**  The current dispatcher allows block N+1's `RustyDrumsProducerTask` to start WRITING `mMultiOutScratch` before block N's 13 `RustyInsertTask` readers have finished CONSUMING it (work-stealing across block boundaries).  Sub-K Serial Fallback bypasses the race by pinning all 14 Rusty tasks to the audio thread (strictly sequential execution → no cross-block scratch race).  QA-DispatcherAffinity's Candidate A scope will close this architectural gap with explicit cross-block barriers OR per-strip arena slot ownership.
- **Carry-Forward Reference §2 (Lock-Free + Lifecycle Primitives) — sfizz's internal threading model continues to be undocumented in BaySickDAW's MT primitive catalog.**  QA-SfzGroup Sub-R/S supplemented §2 with the `std::atomic<int> sequenceCounter_` patch + `fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_relaxed)`; QA-Sfizz confirms the bit-crusher race is NOT inside sfizz's documented state, but elsewhere — either at the BaySickDAW dispatcher level (Candidate A) or at the sfizz internal level (Candidate B 4 sub-mechanisms).  QA-DispatcherAffinity investigation phase will characterize which sub-mechanism is actually firing today + may further supplement §2.
- **CLAUDE.md "Engine audition pattern" — updated.**  The 7-engine `auditionNote(int)` + `std::atomic<int> mAuditionNote { -1 }` pattern documented for all 7 engine processors still holds; the QA-C public `bool isAuditionPending() const noexcept` peek on the 3 sfizz engines (Guitars / Basses / RustyDrums) still holds; QA-Sfizz adds the public `juce::String getKeyswitchLabel(int midiNote) const noexcept` accessor on the same 3 sfizz engines alongside the existing public `setOnAnyStateChange` + `getUndoManager` cluster.  Mirror reference for any future sfizz-engine public accessor work.

### 2026-05-29 23:30 PT — QA-DispatcherAffinity — dispatcher MT-race root-cause: data-driven Candidate B hunt characterized the BaySickRustyDrums bit-crusher as B.5 try-lock-failure strip silencing, cured via Sub-A=(i) engine-lock removal + mProjectLoadInProgress shield raise, then retired the entire Sub-K Serial Fallback band-aid era

**Bucket:** Cross-cutting Infrastructure, Players, Mixer / Routing

#### Done

QA-DispatcherAffinity is the close-spawned follow-up from QA-Sfizz (closed `5079c5d` on 2026-05-28 22:00 PT) — slot per the QA-Sfizz Sub-K Serial Fallback retirement pre-condition + Jeff's slot pick: immediately after QA-Sfizz close, before QA-EngineApvts (Jeff verbatim 2026-05-28 per `feedback_slot_placement_is_spec_call.md`; see §9 fortieth Forks entry).  This batch is the architectural root-cause landing for the BaySickRustyDrums MT-mode bit-crusher distortion on long-sustaining cymbals/hi-hats that QA-Sfizz Task 5 follow-up `0e57fc5` band-aided via the Sub-K Serial Fallback (per-task `mAudioThreadOnly` flag pinning the 3 sfizz-driven engine task families OUT of the MT path) but did NOT root-cause.

**Plan-mode double pivot (§9 forty-first Forks entry).**  The §5 entry + §9 fortieth Forks entry pre-batch framing was Candidate A (cross-block read-write race on `mMultiOutScratch`) primary + Candidate B (per-engine worker affinity, 4 sub-mechanisms B.1 thread-local-state continuity / B.2 non-atomic RR voice swapping / B.3 false sharing / B.4 disk streaming contention) secondary.  Pivot #1 (Jeff at plan-mode entry): global synchronization barrier REJECTED — "A global lock is a massive serialization point that will bottleneck my CPU and artificially degrade the DAW's multi-core headroom" — replaced with a proposed DAG + topological-sort upgrade.  Pivot #2 (Jeff after Phase 1 source verification): the DAG-upgrade framing REJECTED post-exploration — direct reads of [RenderGraphDispatcher.cpp](Source/Engine/RenderGraphDispatcher.cpp) + [VibeThreadPool.cpp](Source/Engine/VibeThreadPool.cpp) + [RenderTask.h](Source/Engine/RenderTask.h) confirmed the dispatcher ALREADY implements dep-driven DAG topological execution (`RenderTask::mDeps` / `mInitialDeps` / `mChildren` / `mPredecessors` + `addSyntheticDep(producer, 13 inserts)` at [PluginProcessor.cpp:4142](Source/PluginProcessor.cpp:4142) + `dispatchBlock` blocks the audio thread on `mAllDone` set by MasterTask so Block N+1 cannot start until Block N completes — the §9 fortieth Candidate A cross-block race hypothesis was structurally impossible against current code).  Reframed to a data-driven sfizz Candidate B bug hunt: Task 1 stands up entry+exit-timestamped trace; Task 2 reviews data + locks Sub-A fix shape; Task 3 implements the cure; Task 4 retires Sub-K conditional on Task 3 cure verify.

Net architectural change: the dispatcher is now PURELY lock-free MT execution as the ONLY production path, with the `mProjectLoadInProgress` shield as the deterministic lifecycle barrier and ZERO per-task audio-thread-affinity flags, ZERO dual-queue MPSC routing, ZERO runtime Sub-K override gate, ZERO RAII trace scopes, and ZERO engine-pointer SpinLocks anywhere in the audio path or the destroy/load mutation windows.  The bit-crusher is cured at the dispatcher layer (not the sfizz-library layer) by removing the per-insert `mRustyDrumsEngineLock` `ScopedTryLockType` that — under MT contention — caused try-lock failures: 13 `RustyInsertTask`s race the single engine spin lock, the losers clear their output buffer + return = intermittent strip silencing audible as the bit-crusher on long sustains.

[RenderEngineFlags.h](Source/Engine/RenderEngineFlags.h) `gSubKOverride` `atomic<bool>` + [VibeThreadPool.cpp](Source/Engine/VibeThreadPool.cpp) `submit()` relaxed-load gate routing to `audioThreadQueue` only when `mAudioThreadOnly && !gSubKOverride` (submit-time gate — chosen over the 3 construction sites Jeff named because tasks are built once on the message thread at kit-load; the named sites get comment-only cross-refs) + [StandaloneEditor.cpp](Source/Standalone/StandaloneEditor.cpp) Mixer hamburger menu item 205 "Sub-K Serial Fallback" flipping the override without rebuild / kit-reload / engine re-registration.  Stage B Sub-K-disabled re-capture (override engaged for the whole capture): **9 worker threads**, **26,912 Release / 42,683 Debug cross-thread within-block overlaps**, **producer-vs-insert overlap = 0** (the synthetic dep is honored — validates the Pivot #2 finding).  Stage C characterized the bit-crusher as **B.5 — try-lock-failure strip silencing**, a NEW sub-mechanism NOT in the §9 fortieth Candidate B B.1-B.4 catalog (all four of which targeted sfizz-library-INTERNAL issues): the actual mechanism is at the BaySickDAW dispatcher layer — `RustyInsertTask::run()` does `juce::SpinLock::ScopedTryLockType lk (mRustyDrumsEngineLock); if (! lk.isLocked()) { mOutputBuffer->clear(); return; }`; under MT the 13 inserts compete for the single engine lock, losers clear + return, and the trace shows the population of sub-µs / 0 ns durations directly (4-84 zero-duration events per channel in Stage B).  Sub-A fix-shape spec call surfaced as a 4-option table (no pre-pick per Rule 5); resolved at Task 3.  Bonus: null-byte CSV dump bug fixed — JUCE `FileOutputStream::writeString` appends a null terminator polluting every CSV row (bash `grep` saw the file as binary); switched to raw-byte `fos.write(text.toRawUTF8(), text.getNumBytesAsUTF8())`.

**Layer 4 — Task 3 cure: Sub-A=(i) lock removal + shield raise + 2 folded findings (`2e14f09`, 6 files +264 / -32).**  Sub-A resolution (Jeff verbatim 2026-05-29): Option (i) — "Remove the spin lock from InsertTasks entirely.  Because the DAG already guarantees the ProducerTask has finished writing to mMultiOutScratch before the 13 InsertTasks are dispatched, the inserts are strictly concurrent readers.  Multiple threads reading a static buffer is perfectly safe.  We do not need, and should not have, an audio-thread lock here.  For lifecycle safety (like engine swapping or kit loading), we will rely on the existing mProjectLoadInProgress barrier on the message thread."  [RustyInsertTask.cpp](Source/Engine/Tasks/RustyInsertTask.cpp) + [RustyDrumsProducerTask.cpp](Source/Engine/Tasks/RustyDrumsProducerTask.cpp) `run()` bodies lose the try-lock + early-return-on-fail (the `engine == nullptr` defensive guard stays).  **Audit-driven Option (A) expansion** (Jeff verbatim "Option (A) Recommended: Lock removal + barrier raising at destroy/load sites... It is perfectly acceptable to have a ~30ms audio dropout when a user explicitly swaps a kit or closes a plugin tab"): the kit-load / engine-swap audit caught that `mRustyDrumsEngine` mutates at 2 message-thread sites NEITHER of which raised the shield pre-Task-3 — `loadBaySickRustyDrumsKit` ran `loadKit(sfzPath)` UNLOCKED (a latent race the pre-Task-3 code tolerated) and `destroyBaySickRustyDrums` relied on the `mRustyDrumsEngineLock` `ScopedLockType` to wait for in-flight try-lock holders (a safety chain that BREAKS once Sub-A=(i) removes the reader try-locks → use-after-free on tab close via [StandaloneEditor.cpp:4217](Source/Standalone/StandaloneEditor.cpp) + program-change via [BaySickRustyDrumsPage.cpp:606](Source/Standalone/BaySickRustyDrumsPage.cpp)).  Fix: raise the `mProjectLoadInProgress` shield + 30 ms sleep + `shieldWasUp` refcount-aware restore around BOTH mutation windows in [PluginProcessor.cpp](Source/PluginProcessor.cpp) (mirrors the `:2948` / `:3183` + `StandaloneEditor.cpp:6266` precedent).  **Folded piano-roll-clear fix** (Jeff Task 3 Verify 2 finding): the tab-delete confirm dialog promises "clear the Rusty piano roll on every pattern" but only `tearDownCurrentProgram` (program-change path) did it; the tab-delete path bypassed the clear — moved the clear INTO `destroyBaySickRustyDrums` (inside the shield window, before the engine reset) as the single source of truth + removed the now-redundant inline loop from [BaySickRustyDrumsPage.cpp](Source/Standalone/BaySickRustyDrumsPage.cpp).  **QA-RustyMeter routed forward** (§9 forty-second Forks entry — landed in this same Task 3 commit per the QA-Sfizz Sub-K `0e57fc5` precedent of mid-batch §9-routing-in-source-commit): Jeff observed at Verify 2 that BaySickRustyDrums AriaControlPanel per-layer-volume CC sliders audibly affect output but the per-strip dbfs meter doesn't reflect it (pre-existing, BaySickRustyDrums-specific — Guitars/Basses verified unaffected); new dedicated batch (fix-shape (2), slot (a) immediately after QA-DispatcherAffinity before QA-EngineApvts), prime suspect `buildOutputRoutedSfzWrapper`.

**Layer 5 — Task 4 Sub-K Serial Fallback retirement full strip pass (`35e6bc3`, 12 files +112 / -550).**  Sub-spec resolved: Option (1) Strip A + B + C + D full clean (Jeff verbatim "Keeping a redundant SpinLock in the audio hot-path as 'belt-and-suspenders' defeats the purpose of the lock-free architecture we just built.  The mProjectLoadInProgress shield is our deterministic barrier; the SpinLock is now dead weight and potential future confusion.").  Four categories, one commit: **Category A** reverts the Task 1 trace infrastructure (`RenderEngineFlags.h` ring + `TraceScope` + `RenderGraphDispatcher.cpp` block bump + the 3 task `run()` TraceScopes + `InstStripTask` `mIsSfizzEngine` + the menu-204 handler) per the Rule 4 catalog `Remove at Task 4 close` disposition; **Category B** reverts the Task 2 `gSubKOverride` gate + menu-205 handler; **Category C** is the source-reverse of QA-Sfizz `0e57fc5` — [RenderTask.h](Source/Engine/RenderTask.h) `mAudioThreadOnly` field, the [VibeThreadPool.cpp](Source/Engine/VibeThreadPool.cpp) `audioThreadQueue` MPSC + all 5 routing/pop/drain sites, the 2 Rusty constructor flag-set lines, and the [PluginProcessor.cpp](Source/PluginProcessor.cpp) `registerInstEngine` Sub-M=(eng-b) `dynamic_cast` flag-set block; **Category D** is the full removal of the pre-existing `mRustyDrumsEngineLock` SpinLock (the declaration at [PluginProcessor.h:840](Source/PluginProcessor.h) + the `prepareToPlay` ScopedLockType + the 2 processBlock MIDI-schedule `ScopedTryLockType` sites + the `loadKit` + `destroy` ScopedLockType wraps — all replaced wholly by the `mProjectLoadInProgress` shield).  The pre-existing QA-Md `MtDiagnostic` counter namespace (gCaptureOn / gBlockCount) was retro-classified `Keep` per Rule 4 (it pre-dates Task 1).  A mid-Task-4 build failure (orphan brace from the pattern-mode try-lock collapse) was caught + fixed before verify.

#### Found along the way

1. **FND-1 (Phase 1 source verification) — the dispatcher already implements dep-driven DAG topological execution.**  `RenderTask::mDeps` / `mInitialDeps` / `mChildren` / `mPredecessors` + `addSyntheticDep(producer, 13 inserts)` at `PluginProcessor.cpp:4142` + `dispatchBlock` blocking on `mAllDone` (set by MasterTask) means Block N+1 cannot start before Block N completes.  The §9 fortieth Candidate A cross-block race hypothesis was structurally impossible — drove Pivot #2 + the reframe to a data-driven Candidate B hunt.

2. **FND-2 (Task 1 Stage A baseline) — Sub-K Serial Fallback empirically confirmed serial.**  All 14 sfizz tasks on a single thread ID per config, ZERO within-block overlap across 20,982 Release / 22,168 Debug transitions.  The Sub-K band-aid does exactly what QA-Sfizz claimed.

3. **FND-3 (Task 2 Stage C) — the bit-crusher is B.5 try-lock-failure strip silencing, a dispatcher-layer mechanism NOT in the §9 fortieth B.1-B.4 catalog.**  The §9 fortieth Candidate B sub-mechanisms all hypothesized sfizz-library-internal causes (thread-local state / RR voice swapping / false sharing / disk streaming).  The trace showed the real cause is the BaySickDAW-level per-insert `mRustyDrumsEngineLock` `ScopedTryLockType`: 13 RustyInsertTasks race the single engine spin lock, losers clear their output buffer + return = intermittent strip silencing on long sustains.  Producer-vs-insert overlap = 0 confirms the synthetic dep is fine; the contention is insert-vs-insert.

4. **FND-4 (Task 2 Stage C) — producer slowdown is a secondary cache-thrashing effect.**  Producer duration grew 1.6× Release / 5.4× Debug under MT-on (spinning try-lock losers thrash the cache); a real but non-primary effect, eliminated by the Task 3 cure (Stage D producer avg improved to 3.8 ms from Stage B's 6.1 ms).

5. **FND-5 (Task 3 cure audit) — `mRustyDrumsEngine` mutates at 2 unshielded message-thread sites.**  `loadBaySickRustyDrumsKit` ran `loadKit` UNLOCKED (latent race the pre-Task-3 try-lock chain tolerated) and `destroyBaySickRustyDrums` relied on the now-removed `mRustyDrumsEngineLock` `ScopedLockType` for reader safety.  Removing the InsertTask try-locks without compensating would have opened a use-after-free on tab close + amplified the latent loadKit race — drove the audit-driven Option (A) shield-raise expansion.

6. **FND-6 (Task 3 Verify 2) — the tab-delete path never cleared the Rusty piano roll despite the confirm dialog's promise.**  Only `tearDownCurrentProgram` (program-change path) did the clear; `closeTab → destroyBaySickRustyDrums` bypassed it.  Pre-existing bug (not a Task 3 regression); fixed in-batch per `feedback_qa_batches_fix_bugs_dont_defer.md` by moving the clear into `destroyBaySickRustyDrums` as single source of truth.

7. **FND-7 (Task 3 Verify 2) — BaySickRustyDrums per-layer-volume CC sliders audibly affect output but the per-strip dbfs meter doesn't reflect it.**  Jeff verbatim "this was something I noticed with sub k on I just hadn't brought it up yet since we were figuring out the bit crusher issue" — pre-existing under Sub-K-on, NOT a Task 3 regression.  BaySickRustyDrums-specific (Guitars/Basses verified unaffected: "the knobs that make it louder do increase their dbfs").  Out-of-scope for QA-DispatcherAffinity (dispatcher MT race, not sfizz CC-vs-meter publish path); routed to a NEW QA-RustyMeter batch.

8. **FND-8 (Task 2 null-byte CSV dump) — JUCE `FileOutputStream::writeString` appends a null terminator.**  Every CSV row got a leading null byte from the prior row's terminator; bash `grep` treated the file as binary (`awk -F, '$1+0 == 100'` returned nothing until `tr -d '\000'`).  Fixed via raw-byte `fos.write` for each row + literal `const char*` header write.

9. **FND-9 (Task 4 post-strip menu inspection) — the QA-Md "Run MT Diagnostic (2s capture)" item 203 + `MtDiagnostic` counter namespace are dead-code-shape retirement candidates.**  QA-Md closed 2026-05-09 with no-bug-found.  Per Rule 4 the item is retro-classified `Keep` (out of Task 4 scope); Jeff confirmed QA-Cleanup-1 ([Main Plan.md:1836](Main Plan.md)) is the natural retirement home (already has 2 dead-code-shape fold-ins).  Routing pick (3) = route at batch close.
